Module `laundry` ("Garment orders", optional, laundry only, on) needs catalog, contacts and sales; permissions `laundry.view`, `laundry.create` (take orders in), `laundry.edit` (the work, the racks, handovers, damage claims recorded), `laundry.cancel` (cancel garments), `laundry.print` (order receipts and tag labels) and `laundry.refund` (decide damage claims; money back). Garment orders are numbered in the series kind `laundry_order` (prefix `LO`; not a GST document). A service is billed as a service item (`catalog_skus`); a garment order's bill is an ordinary sale (`business_documents`, one line a garment), its advance an ordinary receipt (`business_collections`) handed back as a `business_collection_refunds` row of source `laundry`, and what a cancellation or a damage claim gives back an ordinary credit note (`business_returns`) with an ordinary refund (`business_refunds`). The trigger `business_return_lines_laundry` (function `laundry_line_return`) on `business_return_lines` refuses a return of a bill line that charges a garment unless the return's request key is the garment's own `credit_key`.

#### `laundry_settings`
Purpose: how a laundry bills and hands over its orders, and its tag count.
Key columns: PK `business_id`, `bill_at` (delivery, intake; default delivery), `part_delivery` (whole_order, garments; default whole_order), `express_percent` (0-200, default 0), `ready_days` (0-60, default 3), `express_days` (0-60, default 1), `unclaimed_days` (1-365, default 30), `receipt_terms` (up to 2,000 characters), `last_tag_no`, `updated_by`, `updated_at`. The defaults are used until the row is made (on first save, or with the first tag given).
Added by: 1791966000000.

#### `laundry_services`
Purpose: a service on the price list, billed as a service item.
Key columns: `sku_id`, `sort_order`, `active`, `created_by`/`created_at`, `updated_by`/`updated_at`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `sku_id`) -> `catalog_skus`.
Rules: UNIQUE (business, item). Trigger `laundry_services_check`: never deleted; keeps its item, who made it and when; the item is a service of the business, not a kit.
Added by: 1791966000000.

#### `laundry_garment_types`
Purpose: a kind of garment taken in, and how one is counted.
Key columns: `name` (unique index `laundry_garment_types_name` on the business and the name in any case), `counted_as` (piece, pair, set), `pieces` (1-20), `sort_order`, `active`, `created_by`/`created_at`, `updated_by`/`updated_at`.
Rules: CHECK a piece is 1 piece, a pair 2 and a set 2 or more. Trigger `laundry_garment_types_guard`: never deleted; keeps who added it and when.
Added by: 1791966000000.

#### `laundry_prices`
Purpose: the price list: a garment and a service at a price.
Key columns: PK (`business_id`, `garment_type_id`, `service_id`), `per` (item, kg), `price` (> 0), `updated_by`, `updated_at`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `garment_type_id`) -> `laundry_garment_types`; (`business_id`, `service_id`) -> `laundry_services`.
Rules: index `laundry_prices_service`. Changed in place or taken off; each change is in `business_audit` (`laundry_price.changed`).
Added by: 1791966000000.

#### `laundry_orders`
Purpose: a garment order, as it was taken in.
Key columns: `location_id`, `order_no` (series kind `laundry_order`), `series_id`, `contact_id`, `customer_name`, `customer_phone`, `promised_on`, `express`, `express_percent` (0-200), `bill_at`, `part_delivery`, `tax_mode`, `subtotal`, `tax`, `round_off` (between -1 and 1), `total`, `terms`, `notes`, `advance_collection_id`, `document_id`, `request_key`, `request_hash`, `created_by`/`created_at`, `updated_by`/`updated_at`.
Relations: (`business_id`, ...) -> `business_locations`, `business_number_series`, `business_contacts`, `business_collections` (the advance) and `business_documents` (the bill made at intake).
Rules:
- CHECK `total` = `subtotal` + `tax` + `round_off`; an express percentage only when express; a bill exactly when billed at intake; an advance only when billed at delivery.
- UNIQUE (business, order number), (business, request key), (business, bill) and (business, advance); indexes `laundry_orders_period`, `laundry_orders_contact` and `laundry_orders_promised`.
- Trigger `laundry_orders_customer` (function `business_contact_is_customer`).
- Trigger `laundry_orders_guard`: never deleted; only `promised_on`, `notes`, `updated_by` and `updated_at` change.
Added by: 1791966000000; 1791977000000 (`laundry_orders_guard` lets an erasure's change through: `business_privacy_erasing`).

#### `laundry_order_items`
Purpose: each garment of an order (or several of a kind under one tag, or a bundle by the kilo), from intake to the handover.
Key columns: `order_id`, `seq` (1-200), `tag_no` (`^[A-Z0-9][A-Z0-9/-]{0,19}$`), `garment_type_id`, `service_id`, `garment_name`, `service_name`, `sku_id`, `counted_as`, `per` (item, kg), `quantity` (above 0, under 1,000; whole unless by the kilo), `pieces` (1-999), `rate`, `base`, `surcharge`, `amount`, `tax_rate`, `net`, `tax`, `colour`, `brand`, `defects` (TEXT[] of stain, tear, hole, missing_button, colour_bleed, fading, shrinkage, burn, worn, loose_thread), `defect_note`, `instructions`, `risk_note`, `acknowledged`, `declared_value` (> 0), `status` (received, in_process, ready, delivered, cancelled), `rack` (`^[A-Z0-9][A-Z0-9 /-]{0,19}$`), `ready_at`, `ready_by`, `delivered_at`, `delivered_by`, `cancel_reason`, `cancelled_by`, `cancelled_at`, `cancel_key`, `document_id`, `line_id`, `credit_key`, `created_at`.
Relations: (`business_id`, ...) -> `laundry_orders`, `laundry_garment_types`, `laundry_services`, `catalog_skus`, `business_documents` and `business_document_lines`.
Rules:
- CHECK `amount` = `base` + `surcharge`; `net` = `amount` (prices before GST) or `net` + `tax` = `amount` (including GST); acknowledged only with defects, a note on them or a risk note; a rack only when ready or delivered; ready and delivered times, who and when, and the cancellation's reason, who and when exactly with their status; a cancel key only when cancelled; a bill and a line together; a credit key only with a line.
- UNIQUE (business, order, seq) and (business, line); unique index `laundry_order_items_open_tag` (a tag on one garment received, in process or ready at a time); indexes `laundry_order_items_order`, `laundry_order_items_tag`, the partial `laundry_order_items_open` and `laundry_order_items_document`.
- Trigger `laundry_order_items_insert_check`: written only in the transaction that takes its order in; starts received with nothing delivered, cancelled or credited; its item is its service's and its counting its garment's; on an order billed at intake, on a line of the order's own bill, of its item.
- Trigger `laundry_order_items_guard`: never deleted; keeps what was noted and priced; a cancelled one never changes; moves only received → in_process, ready or cancelled; in_process → ready or cancelled; ready → in_process, delivered or cancelled; delivered → received only with a damage claim decided `reprocess` in the same transaction; delivered only with its bill line and in a handover made in the same transaction; a billed one cancelled only once its line has a return; its line (of its own item, on a sale to the order's customer) and its credit key given once; re-tagged only while in the shop or when taken back in.
Added by: 1791966000000.

#### `laundry_deliveries`
Purpose: each handover of an order's garments.
Key columns: `order_id`, `document_id` (the bill posted with it, when billed at delivery), `on_account`, `collected_by`, `note`, `request_key`, `request_hash`, `created_by`, `created_at`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `order_id`) -> `laundry_orders`; (`business_id`, `document_id`) -> `business_documents`.
Rules: UNIQUE (business, request key) and (business, bill); index `laundry_deliveries_order`; trigger `laundry_deliveries_append_only` (function `laundry_append_only`).
Added by: 1791966000000.

#### `laundry_delivery_items`
Purpose: the garments of each handover.
Key columns: PK (`business_id`, `delivery_id`, `item_id`), `order_id`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `delivery_id`) -> `laundry_deliveries`; (`business_id`, `order_id`, `item_id`) -> `laundry_order_items`.
Rules: index `laundry_delivery_items_item`; trigger `laundry_delivery_items_check`: of the handover's own order; trigger `laundry_delivery_items_append_only` (function `laundry_append_only`).
Added by: 1791966000000.

#### `laundry_order_events`
Purpose: everything that happened to an order or one of its garments.
Key columns: `order_id`, `item_id` (none for the order), `from_status`, `to_status`, `note`, `created_by`, `created_at`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `order_id`) -> `laundry_orders`; (`business_id`, `order_id`, `item_id`) -> `laundry_order_items`.
Rules: CHECK a status or a note; a from status only with a to status. Index `laundry_order_events_order`; trigger `laundry_order_events_append_only` (function `laundry_append_only`).
Added by: 1791966000000.

#### `laundry_notices`
Purpose: each message or call to the customer about an order.
Key columns: `order_id`, `kind` (received, ready, delayed, reminder), `channel` (whatsapp, call, sms), `created_by`, `created_at`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `order_id`) -> `laundry_orders`.
Rules: index `laundry_notices_order`; trigger `laundry_notices_append_only` (function `laundry_append_only`).
Added by: 1791966000000.

#### `laundry_claims`
Purpose: a damage claim on a garment, and a manager's decision on it.
Key columns: `order_id`, `item_id`, `issue` (damage, stain, shrinkage, colour_run, lost, missing_part, other), `description`, `asked_amount` (> 0), `reported_by`, `reported_at`, `request_key`, `request_hash`, `decision` (refund, credit, reprocess, rejected), `amount` (> 0), `decision_note`, `decided_by`, `decided_at`, `decide_key`, `decide_hash`, `return_id`, `refund_id`, `refunded` (> 0).
Relations: (`business_id`, `order_id`) -> `laundry_orders`; (`business_id`, `order_id`, `item_id`) -> `laundry_order_items`; (`business_id`, `return_id`) -> `business_returns`; `refund_id` -> `business_refunds`.
Rules:
- CHECK nothing decided without a decision; the decision exactly with who, when and its request (key and hash); an amount and a credit note exactly on money back or a credit note; a refund only on money back, with its amount; a note when rejected.
- UNIQUE (business, request key) and (business, decide key); unique index `laundry_claims_one_open` (one claim waiting a garment); indexes `laundry_claims_order` and the partial `laundry_claims_open`.
- Trigger `laundry_claims_check`: never deleted; on a garment not cancelled; starts waiting; decided once, then never changes; keeps what was reported.
Added by: 1791966000000.

## Jewellery & gold

A jeweller's own tables (type `jewellery`), added by 1791967000000 jewellery. Module `jewellery` ("Gold rates & jewellery", optional, jewellery only, on) needs catalog, contacts, sales, inventory and serials (serial numbers start on for a jeweller); permissions `jewellery.view`, `jewellery.create`, `jewellery.edit`, `jewellery.approve` (set the day's rates; pay out old gold; set up scheme plans; approve stopping a scheme), `jewellery.cancel` (cancel old gold; stop a scheme), `jewellery.print` and `jewellery.export`; the type-only role template `karigar`. The function `jewellery_fineness(metal, purity)` gives the fineness of each purity a jeweller deals in (NULL for any other), `jewellery_current_rate(business, metal, purity)` the rate of the latest sheet of the business's date that rates it, and `jewellery_money(numerator, denominator)` a quotient in rupees to the paisa, halves up, worked out exactly. A piece is a serial-numbered unit of its jewellery item: a `business_stock_lots` row of the item with the tag as its `lot_code` and quantity 1, received on a purchase or brought in as a `business_stock_adjustments` row of +1 with its reason (the core's `receiveSerial`), and taken out by a −1 correction when it is melted. `jewellery_plain_words(text)` is false for text with "lab grown", "lab created", "lab diamond" or "LGD" in it.

#### `jewellery_rate_sheets`
Purpose: each sheet of rates a manager sets.
Key columns: `rate_date` (the business's date when it was set), `session` (morning, evening), `source` (shop, association, ibja), `note`, `request_key`, `request_hash`, `created_by`, `created_at`.
Rules: UNIQUE (business, request key); index `jewellery_rate_sheets_day`; trigger `jewellery_rate_sheets_day` sets `rate_date` from `created_at` in the business's time zone; trigger `jewellery_rate_sheets_append_only` (function `jewellery_append_only`).
Added by: 1791967000000; 1791974000000 (live updates: `jewellery_rate_sheets_live`).

#### `jewellery_rates`
Purpose: each purity's rates on a sheet.
Key columns: PK (`business_id`, `sheet_id`, `metal`, `purity`), `metal` (gold, silver, platinum), `purity` (24K, 22K, 18K, 14K; 999, 925; 950), `sell_rate` (per gram, > 0), `buy_rate` (per gram for old metal, > 0 or NULL).
Relations: (`business_id`, `sheet_id`) -> `jewellery_rate_sheets`.
Rules: CHECK `jewellery_fineness(metal, purity)` is not NULL; index `jewellery_rates_purity`; trigger `jewellery_rates_check`: only in the transaction that sets its sheet; trigger `jewellery_rates_append_only` (function `jewellery_append_only`).
Added by: 1791967000000.

#### `jewellery_settings`
Purpose: how a jeweller's tags are numbered, and the hallmarking charge a new piece starts with.
Key columns: PK `business_id`, `tag_prefix` (0 to 6 letters; default T), `last_tag_no`, `hallmark_charge` (0-10,000; default 0), `old_gold_deduction` (0 to below 100; default 0), `old_gold_cash_limit` (the shop's own limit on cash paid out for old gold to one seller in a day: above 0 and below 2,00,000, or none), `cash_watch_level` (the cash report's own alert level: above 0 and below 10,00,000, or none), `reporting_entity`, `reporting_entity_by`, `reporting_entity_at` (CHECK: said by someone, at a time), `updated_by`, `updated_at`. The defaults are used until the row is made (on first save, or with the first tag given).
Added by: 1791967000000.

#### `jewellery_items`
Purpose: a catalogue item sold as tagged pieces priced by weight, and what a new piece of it starts with.
Key columns: PK (`business_id`, `sku_id`), `metal`, `purity`, `wastage_percent` (0-100), `making_mode` (per_gram, percent, flat), `making_value` (≥ 0; at most 100 as a percentage), `created_by`, `updated_by`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `sku_id`) -> `catalog_skus`.
Rules: CHECK a purity of the metal; trigger `jewellery_items_check`: goods counted in pieces (a unit of kind count), serial-numbered, at the 3% slab, a name free of the disallowed words for a man-made diamond; keeps its item; never deleted.
Added by: 1791967000000.

#### `jewellery_pieces`
Purpose: a tagged piece: a serial-numbered unit of its jewellery item in stock, its tag the serial number.
Key columns: `sku_id`, `tag_no` (letters, digits, / and -, up to 20), `description`, `metal`, `purity`, `gross_weight` (> 0), `stone_weight` (≥ 0, below the gross), `net_weight` (generated: gross less stones), `wastage_percent`, `making_mode`, `making_value`, `hallmarked`, `hallmark_charge` (0 when not hallmarked), `exemption` (gold only), `status` (in_stock, on_approval, at_karigar, sold, melted), `with_whom` (exactly while on approval or at the karigar), `notes`, `request_key`, `request_hash`, `created_by`, `updated_by`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `sku_id`) -> `jewellery_items`.
Rules: UNIQUE (business, tag) and (business, request key); CHECK words free of the disallowed terms (`jewellery_plain_words`); indexes `jewellery_pieces_item`, `jewellery_pieces_status`; trigger `jewellery_pieces_guard`: tagged on a unit of its item in stock, starts in stock; keeps its item and tag; never deleted; in stock → on approval, at the karigar or melted, and back; melted once its unit has left stock; a sold or melted piece keeps what it was; hallmarked while it has a HUID.
Added by: 1791967000000.

#### `jewellery_piece_huids`
Purpose: each HUID of a piece's hallmark.
Key columns: PK (`business_id`, `huid` — 6 letters and digits), `piece_id`, `created_by`, `created_at`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `piece_id`) -> `jewellery_pieces`.
Rules: index `jewellery_piece_huids_piece`; trigger `jewellery_piece_huids_check` (function `jewellery_piece_parts_check`): only on a hallmarked piece in the shop; never changed.
Added by: 1791967000000.

#### `jewellery_piece_stones`
Purpose: each stone line of a piece.
Key columns: PK (`business_id`, `piece_id`, `seq`), `kind`, `description`, `pieces` (1-10,000), `carat` (> 0), `grade`, `certificate`, `treatment`, `value` (≥ 0).
Relations: (`business_id`, `piece_id`) -> `jewellery_pieces`.
Rules: CHECK words free of the disallowed terms, and a diamond's description never "fake" or "artificial"; trigger `jewellery_piece_stones_check` (function `jewellery_piece_parts_check`): only while the piece is in the shop.
Added by: 1791967000000.

#### `jewellery_piece_events`
Purpose: everything that happened to a piece, as written.
Key columns: `piece_id`, `kind` (tagged, changed, sent, back, sold, returned, melted), `from_status`, `to_status`, `note`, `details` (JSON object), `request_key` (a change of status), `created_by`, `created_at`.
Rules: UNIQUE (business, request key); index `jewellery_piece_events_piece`; trigger `jewellery_piece_events_append_only` (function `jewellery_append_only`).
Added by: 1791967000000.

#### `jewellery_locations`
Purpose: whether a location's district is under mandatory hallmarking, as the owner says.
Key columns: PK (`business_id`, `location_id`), `district`, `mandatory_hallmarking`, `confirmed_by`, `confirmed_at`. A location with no row is taken as under mandatory hallmarking. `jewellery_settings` also has `turnover_exempt` with `turnover_confirmed_by` and `turnover_confirmed_at` (CHECK: an exemption is said by someone).
Relations: (`business_id`, `location_id`) -> `business_locations`.
Added by: 1791967000000.

#### `jewellery_scheme_plans`
Purpose: a gold scheme the shop offers.
Key columns: `name` (UNIQUE per business, ignoring case), `instalments` (2-11), `min_instalment` (100 to 10,00,000, whole rupees), `discount_percent` (0-100, of one instalment), `terms` (up to 4,000 characters), `active`, `request_key`, `request_hash`, `created_by`, `created_at`, `updated_by`, `updated_at`.
Rules: CHECKs (no "interest" in the name or terms); UNIQUE (business, request key); index `jewellery_scheme_plans_name`; trigger `jewellery_scheme_plans_guard`: never deleted; only `active` changes.
Added by: 1791967000000.

#### `jewellery_schemes`
Purpose: a customer's gold scheme.
Key columns: `location_id`, `scheme_no` (UNIQUE per business; number series kind gold_scheme), `series_id`, `contact_id`, `member_name`, `member_phone`, `plan_id`, `plan_name`, `instalments`, `instalment` (whole rupees), `discount_percent`, `terms`, `nominee_name`, `nominee_relation`, `terms_accepted_at`, `started_on`, `maturity_on`, `close_by`, `status` (active, stopped, redeemed), `stop_reason`, `stopped_by`, `stop_approved_by`, `stopped_at`, `redeemed_document_id`, `redeemed_at`, `discount`, `notes`, `request_key`, `request_hash`, `created_by`, `created_at`.
Relations: location; contact; (`business_id`, `plan_id`) -> `jewellery_scheme_plans`; series; (`business_id`, `redeemed_document_id`) -> `business_documents`.
Rules: CHECKs (maturity = start + instalments months; close by = start + 364 days; the stop's four columns together; redeemed with its bill, time and discount); UNIQUE (business, request key); indexes `jewellery_schemes_contact`, partial `jewellery_schemes_open`; trigger `jewellery_schemes_customer` (function `business_contact_is_customer`); trigger `jewellery_schemes_check`: joined today, active, on an active plan with its terms as they are and at least its smallest instalment; trigger `jewellery_schemes_guard`: keeps what it was joined with; never deleted; stopped once from active; redeemed once by a jewellery bill of its member made with it, with the discount earned (every instalment paid, not stopped, on or after maturity); constraint trigger `jewellery_schemes_redeemed` (deferred to the end of the transaction): its instalments' receipts all pay that bill.
Added by: 1791967000000; 1791977000000 (`jewellery_schemes_guard` lets an erasure's change through: `business_privacy_erasing`).

#### `jewellery_scheme_instalments`
Purpose: each instalment of a gold scheme, paid.
Key columns: PK (`business_id`, `scheme_id`, `seq` 1-11), `due_on`, `collection_id` (UNIQUE: the core collection receipt), `amount`, `paid_on`, `created_by`, `created_at`.
Relations: scheme; (`business_id`, `collection_id`) -> `business_collections`.
Rules: trigger `jewellery_scheme_instalments_check`: the next of an active scheme before its maturity, its monthly amount and due day, on the business's date, the member's own receipt (not a credit note) made with it, and the cash into the scheme under ₹2,00,000 (a sentence citing section 186 of the Income-tax Act 2025); trigger `jewellery_scheme_instalments_append_only` (function `jewellery_append_only`). On `business_collection_refunds`, trigger `business_collection_refunds_jewellery_scheme` (function `jewellery_scheme_refund_check`) refuses to refund a scheme instalment's receipt.
Added by: 1791967000000.

#### `jewellery_bills`
Purpose: a sale of tagged pieces posted by the jewellery bill, with the rules it was billed under.
Key columns: `document_id` (the core sale), `location_id`, `contact_id` (none for a walk-in), `rate_date`, `hallmark_mandatory`, `turnover_exempt`, `scheme_id` (the gold scheme it redeems; UNIQUE per business), `scheme_discount` (≥ 0; nil without a scheme), `notes`, `request_key`, `request_hash`, `created_by`, `created_at`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `document_id`) -> `business_documents`; location; contact; (`business_id`, `scheme_id`) -> `jewellery_schemes`.
Rules: UNIQUE (business, sale) and (business, request key); index `jewellery_bills_period`; trigger `jewellery_bills_check`: its own sale posted in the same transaction at its location to its customer, the business's date, the location's hallmarking rule and the turnover exemption as they are, a scheme of its own customer not yet redeemed; constraint trigger `jewellery_bills_discount` (deferred to the end of the transaction): its lines carry its scheme's discount and no other; trigger `jewellery_bills_append_only` (function `jewellery_append_only`).
Added by: 1791967000000.

#### `jewellery_bill_lines`
Purpose: each piece on a jewellery bill as it was billed.
Key columns: PK (`business_id`, `line_id` — the sale's line), `bill_id`, `piece_id`, `tag_no`, `description`, `metal`, `purity`, `gross_weight`, `stone_weight`, `net_weight`, `rate_sheet_id`, `rate`, `metal_value`, `wastage_percent`, `wastage_weight`, `wastage_value`, `making_mode`, `making_value`, `making_charge`, `stone_value`, `stones` (JSON array), `hallmarked`, `hallmark_charge`, `huids` (TEXT[]), `hallmark_basis` (huid, not_gold, under_2g, turnover, district, or an exemption), `value`, `discount`, `taxable`, `tax_rate` (3), `tax`.
Relations: bill; (`business_id`, `line_id`) -> `business_document_lines`; piece; (`business_id`, `rate_sheet_id`) -> `jewellery_rate_sheets`.
Rules: CHECKs on every figure (net = gross − stones; metal = round(net × rate, 2); wastage weight = round(net × wastage% ÷ 100, 3); wastage = round(net × rate × wastage% ÷ 100, 2); making per mode; value = metal + wastage + making + stones + hallmarking; discount within the value; taxable = value − discount; GST = round(taxable × 3 ÷ 100, 2); no hallmarking charge or HUID on a piece not hallmarked); indexes `jewellery_bill_lines_bill`, `jewellery_bill_lines_piece`; trigger `jewellery_bill_lines_check`: on a line of the bill's sale (the piece's item and tag, one unit, at the line's taxable value and GST), the piece in stock as it is, the latest rate of the day for its purity, and the HUID check; trigger `jewellery_bill_lines_append_only` (function `jewellery_append_only`).
Added by: 1791967000000.

#### `jewellery_old_gold`
Purpose: an old gold voucher: gold, silver or platinum a customer sells to the shop or gives in exchange.
Key columns: `location_id`, `voucher_no` (UNIQUE per business; number series kind old_gold), `series_id`, `contact_id`, `seller_name`, `seller_phone`, `id_kind` (aadhaar, voter_id, driving_licence, passport, pan_card, other), `id_ref` (2 to 30 letters, digits, / and -; of an Aadhaar card its last four digits only), `pan`, `pan_declaration`, `owner_declared` (always true), `rate_date`, `net_weight` (> 0), `fine_weight`, `round_off` (−0.49 to 0.50, leaving whole rupees), `value` (> 0), `notes`, `request_key`, `request_hash`, `created_by`, `created_at`, `cancelled_at`, `cancelled_by`, `cancel_reason`.
Relations: location; (`business_id`, `contact_id`) -> `business_contacts`; series -> `business_number_series`.
Rules: CHECKs (a PAN or a Form 97 declaration over ₹2,00,000, not both; the cancellation's three columns together); UNIQUE (business, request key); indexes `jewellery_old_gold_period`, `jewellery_old_gold_contact`; trigger `jewellery_old_gold_customer` (function `business_contact_is_customer`); trigger `jewellery_old_gold_check`: made now, on the business's date; trigger `jewellery_old_gold_guard`: never deleted, only cancelled, once, while nothing is paid out or used on it; constraint trigger `jewellery_old_gold_totals` (deferred to the end of the transaction): its weights and value are its lines'.
Added by: 1791967000000.

#### `jewellery_old_gold_lines`
Purpose: each article on an old gold voucher, as tested and valued.
Key columns: PK (`business_id`, `old_gold_id`, `seq` 1-30), `description`, `pieces`, `metal`, `purity`, `test_method` (xrf, touchstone, acid, fire_assay), `tested_fineness` (> 0, below 1,000), `gross_weight`, `less_weight`, `net_weight` (generated), `fine_weight`, `huid`, `rate_sheet_id`, `buy_rate`, `deduction_percent` (0 to below 100), `value`.
Relations: voucher; (`business_id`, `rate_sheet_id`) -> `jewellery_rate_sheets`.
Rules: CHECKs (stones and dirt below the gross weight; fine weight = round(net × fineness found ÷ 1,000, 3); value = `jewellery_money`(net × buy rate × fineness found × (100 − deduction), the purity's fineness × 100)); trigger `jewellery_old_gold_lines_check`: on its voucher when it is made, at the latest buy rate of the day for its purity; trigger `jewellery_old_gold_lines_append_only` (function `jewellery_append_only`).
Added by: 1791967000000.

#### `jewellery_old_gold_uses`
Purpose: an old gold voucher's value used on a jewellery bill, or put back from it when the bill was returned.
Key columns: `old_gold_id`, `document_id`, `payment_id` (a use: the bill's credit-note payment; `amount` > 0), `refund_id` (a put-back: the bill's credit-note refund; `amount` < 0), `created_by`, `created_at`.
Relations: voucher; document; (`business_id`, `payment_id`) -> `business_payments`; `refund_id` -> `business_refunds`.
Rules: UNIQUE payment and refund; indexes `jewellery_old_gold_uses_voucher`, `jewellery_old_gold_uses_document`; trigger `jewellery_old_gold_uses_check`: a voucher not cancelled, on a jewellery bill of the customer who brought it in; a use is the bill's credit-note payment of the same amount with the voucher's number, made with it; a put-back the bill's credit-note refund of the amount, made with it, never more than the voucher paid on the bill; no more used and paid out than the voucher is worth; trigger `jewellery_old_gold_uses_append_only` (function `jewellery_append_only`).
Added by: 1791967000000.

#### `jewellery_old_gold_payouts`
Purpose: an old gold voucher's value paid out in money.
Key columns: `old_gold_id`, `expense_id`, `amount` (> 0), `method` (cash, upi, bank), `reference`, `request_key`, `request_hash`, `created_by`, `created_at`.
Relations: voucher; (`business_id`, `expense_id`) -> `business_expenses`.
Rules: UNIQUE expense and (business, request key); indexes `jewellery_old_gold_payouts_voucher`, partial `jewellery_old_gold_payouts_day` (cash); trigger `jewellery_old_gold_payouts_check`: a voucher not cancelled; its own expense of the same amount and method at the voucher's location, made with it, not reversed; no more used and paid out than the voucher is worth; cash to the seller in a business day, and on the voucher, under ₹2,00,000 (a sentence citing section 186 of the Income-tax Act 2025), and within the shop's own limit; trigger `jewellery_old_gold_payouts_append_only` (function `jewellery_append_only`).
Added by: 1791967000000.

#### `jewellery_metal_movements`
Purpose: the shop's own metal by purity at a location, for melting and refining.
Key columns: `location_id`, `metal`, `purity`, `net_weight` (> 0 in, < 0 out), `fine_weight` (the same sign, or 0), `source` (melted_piece; old_gold; old_gold_cancelled), `piece_id`, `old_gold_id` and `old_gold_seq`, `note`, `created_by`, `created_at`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `location_id`) -> `business_locations`; (`business_id`, `piece_id`) -> `jewellery_pieces`; (`business_id`, `old_gold_id`, `old_gold_seq`) -> `jewellery_old_gold_lines`.
Rules: CHECKs (a melted piece's movement names it, old gold's names its line; only a voucher's cancellation goes out); index `jewellery_metal_movements_stock`; unique indexes `jewellery_metal_movements_piece` (a melted piece's metal once) and `jewellery_metal_movements_old_gold` (each line in once and out once); trigger `jewellery_metal_movements_check`: a melted piece's own purity, net weight and fine weight (net × fineness ÷ 1,000, to three places); old gold's line's own purity, weights and location, in when its voucher is made and out when it is cancelled; trigger `jewellery_metal_movements_append_only` (function `jewellery_append_only`).
Added by: 1791967000000.

## Rentals & hire

A rental and hire business's own tables (type `rentals`), added by 1791968000000 rentals. Module `rentals` ("Hire items & bookings", optional, rentals only, on) needs catalog, contacts and sales; permissions `rentals.view`, `rentals.create` (quotes and bookings), `rentals.edit` (change and extend bookings, hand over and take back, units in and out of service), `rentals.approve` (less deposit than asked), `rentals.cancel`, `rentals.refund` (hand back deposits and advances) and `rentals.print`. *Licences & registrations* is recommended to the type. Bookings are numbered in the series kind `rental` (prefix `RN`; not a GST document). A rental item is billed as a service item (`catalog_skus`); a booking's bill is an ordinary sale (`business_documents`), its advances and deposits ordinary receipts (`business_collections`) that pay that bill (`business_payments` with `collection_id`), and what is left of them is handed back as `business_collection_refunds` rows of source `rentals`. How they are used: [BUSINESS_PLATFORM_DATA_MODEL.md › Rentals & hire](BUSINESS_PLATFORM_DATA_MODEL.md#rentals--hire).

#### `rental_settings`
Purpose: whether a hirer's photo ID is recorded at every handover, and the terms on the slips.
Key columns: PK `business_id`, `id_required` (default true), `terms` (up to 2,000 characters, not blank), `updated_by`, `updated_at`. The defaults are used until the row is made (on first save).
Added by: 1791968000000.

#### `rental_items`
Purpose: each thing hired out, its rates and rules.
Key columns: `sku_id` (the service item its hire is billed as), `kind` (unit, pool), `pool_quantity` (0-1,000,000; a pool only), `vehicle` (car, motorcycle, other), `with_driver`, `meter` (km, hours), `description`, `deposit` (>= 0, for each one), `rate_hour`, `rate_day`, `rate_week`, `rate_month` (> 0), `min_hours` (0-2,160), `buffer_minutes` (the turnaround time, 0-10,080), `grace_minutes` (0-1,440), `late_per` (none, hour, day), `late_rate` (> 0), `sort_order`, `active`, `created_by`/`created_at`, `updated_by`/`updated_at`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `sku_id`) -> `catalog_skus`.
Rules:
- CHECK at least one rate; a pool quantity exactly for a pool; a vehicle and a meter only for a unit item, a vehicle's meter km; with a driver only for a vehicle; no late rate when there is no late fee, and a late fee by the hour or the day has its own rate or the hour or day rate.
- UNIQUE (business, item); index `rental_items_list`.
- Trigger `rental_items_guard`: the item is a service of the business counted in services and not a kit; it keeps its item, kind and vehicle, who made it and when; never deleted.
Added by: 1791968000000.

#### `rental_units`
Purpose: each unit of an item hired one by one.
Key columns: `item_id`, `label`, `serial_no` (capital letters, digits, `/` and `-`, up to 30), `meter_reading` (>= 0, one place), `status` (available, maintenance, retired), `status_note`, `created_by`/`created_at`, `updated_by`/`updated_at`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `item_id`) -> `rental_items`.
Rules:
- CHECK a note unless available; unique indexes `rental_units_serial` (business, number) and `rental_units_label` (business, item, name in any case).
- Trigger `rental_units_guard`: of an item hired one by one; a vehicle's unit has its registration; keeps its item, who made it and when; the meter never goes back; never deleted.
Added by: 1791968000000.

#### `rental_bookings`
Purpose: a quote or a booking, from its out time to its due back time.
Key columns: `location_id`, `booking_no` (series kind `rental`), `series_id`, `contact_id`, `customer_name`, `customer_phone`, `status` (quote, reserved, out, returned, cancelled), `out_at`, `due_at`, `tax_mode`, `subtotal`, `tax`, `round_off` (between -1 and 1), `total`, `deposit_due`, `notes`, `checked_out_at`, `checked_out_by`, `checkout_key`, `checkout_hash`, `id_kind` (aadhaar, voter_id, driving_licence, passport, pan_card, other), `id_ref`, `licence_no`, `licence_valid_until`, `deposit_waived_by`, `deposit_waiver`, `returned_at`, `document_id`, `cancelled_at`, `cancelled_by`, `cancel_reason`, `cancel_key`, `cancel_hash`, `request_key`, `request_hash`, `created_by`/`created_at`, `updated_by`/`updated_at`.
Relations: (`business_id`, ...) -> `business_locations`, `business_number_series`, `business_contacts` and `business_documents` (the bill).
Rules:
- CHECK due after out and at most 90 days after; `total` = `subtotal` + `tax` + `round_off`; the handover's columns exactly when out or returned, an ID kind with its number (of an Aadhaar four digits, `id_ref` capital letters, digits, `/` and `-`), a licence number (5-30 of the same) with its date, less deposit with who agreed; `returned_at` exactly when returned, with a bill; a bill only when returned or cancelled; the cancellation's columns exactly when cancelled.
- UNIQUE (business, booking number), (business, request key), (business, bill), (business, handover key), (business, cancellation key); indexes `rental_bookings_period`, `rental_bookings_status`, `rental_bookings_contact` and `rental_bookings_due`.
- Trigger `rental_bookings_customer` (function `business_contact_is_customer`).
- Trigger `rental_bookings_insert_check`: a quote or reserved, out today or later on the business's clock, made when saved.
- Trigger `rental_bookings_guard`: never deleted; keeps its number, hirer, location and request; quote → reserved or cancelled, reserved → out or cancelled, out → returned; the out time changes only before the handover, never to a day that has passed; what is recorded at the handover is written with it, when every unit is in service, the deposit held (`rental_deposit_held`) is the one asked unless someone agreed to less, and a self-drive vehicle has a driving licence in force that day; billed once, on a sale to the hirer; returned or cancelled, it never changes.
- Constraint trigger `rental_bookings_available` (deferred): when it is reserved, handed over or its times change, every line that holds something is checked (`rental_check_booking`).
Added by: 1791968000000; 1791977000000 (`rental_bookings_guard` lets an erasure's change through: `business_privacy_erasing`).

#### `rental_booking_lines`
Purpose: each item on a booking, priced and as handed over.
Key columns: `booking_id`, `seq` (1-50), `item_id`, `unit_id`, `sku_id`, `item_name`, `unit_label`, `serial_no`, `quantity` (1-1,000,000), `rate_hour`, `rate_day`, `rate_week`, `rate_month`, `min_hours`, `buffer_minutes`, `grace_minutes`, `late_per`, `late_rate`, `deposit_each`, `charged_hours` (1-2,160), `months`, `weeks`, `days`, `hours`, `price`, `amount`, `tax_rate`, `net`, `tax`, `out_note`, `meter_out`, `fuel_out` (empty, quarter, half, three_quarters, full), `line_id`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `booking_id`) -> `rental_bookings`; (`business_id`, `item_id`) -> `rental_items`; (`business_id`, `item_id`, `unit_id`) -> `rental_units`; (`business_id`, `sku_id`) -> `catalog_skus`; (`business_id`, `line_id`) -> `business_document_lines`.
Rules:
- CHECK a unit's line is one; a unit with its name; at least one rate; a late rate exactly when there is a late fee; each block of the mix has its rate; the blocks (720, 168, 24 and 1 hours) cover the hours charged; `price` = the blocks times their rates; `amount` = `price` × `quantity`; `net` = `amount`, or `net` + `tax` = `amount`.
- UNIQUE (business, booking, place), (business, bill line); unique indexes `rental_booking_lines_one_unit` and `rental_booking_lines_one_pool` (a unit once a booking, a pool item on one line); indexes `rental_booking_lines_item` and `rental_booking_lines_unit`.
- Trigger `rental_booking_lines_guard`: added to a quote or a reserved booking, of its item's service item, by its unit (not retired) or how many, its item in use, with nothing handed over or billed; keeps its item and unit; how many changes only before the handover, the prices only until it is back; what is noted at the handover is written once, with the handover; the bill line is given once, a line of its own item on a sale to the hirer; taken off only before the handover.
- Constraint trigger `rental_booking_lines_available` (deferred, on insert and a change of how many): `rental_check_line`.
Added by: 1791968000000.

#### `rental_receipts`
Purpose: the advances and refundable deposits taken for a booking.
Key columns: PK (`business_id`, `collection_id`), `booking_id`, `purpose` (advance, deposit), `created_by`, `created_at`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `collection_id`) -> `business_collections`; (`business_id`, `booking_id`) -> `rental_bookings`.
Rules: index `rental_receipts_booking`. Trigger `rental_receipts_check`: the booking is reserved or out, the receipt is new, in the hirer's name, by cash, card, UPI or bank. Trigger `rental_receipts_append_only` (function `rental_append_only`). Function `rental_deposit_held(business, booking)`: what the deposit receipts took, less what paid the bill and what was handed back.
Added by: 1791968000000.

#### `rental_returns`
Purpose: each time things come back.
Key columns: `booking_id`, `final`, `document_id` (the bill), `note`, `request_key`, `request_hash`, `created_by`, `created_at` (when it came back).
Relations: (`business_id`, `booking_id`) -> `rental_bookings`; (`business_id`, `document_id`) -> `business_documents`.
Rules: CHECK a bill exactly when final; UNIQUE (business, request key); index `rental_returns_booking`. Trigger `rental_returns_check`: of a booking out, when written, its bill a sale to the hirer. Trigger `rental_returns_append_only` (function `rental_append_only`).
Added by: 1791968000000.

#### `rental_return_lines`
Purpose: what came back of each line.
Key columns: PK (`business_id`, `return_id`, `line_id`), `booking_id`, `quantity`, `missing`, `note`, `meter_in`, `fuel_in`, `late_minutes`, `late_units`, `late_fee`, `late_line_id`, `maintenance`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `booking_id`, `return_id`) -> `rental_returns`; (`business_id`, `booking_id`, `line_id`) -> `rental_booking_lines`; (`business_id`, `late_line_id`) -> `business_document_lines`.
Rules: CHECK something back or missing; late units exactly with a late fee; a bill line only with a late fee; UNIQUE (business, bill line); index `rental_return_lines_line`. Trigger `rental_return_lines_guard`: no more than is still out of the line; a meter not below the handover's; the late minutes from the due time to now, the units after the grace time (each hour or day, or part of one) and the fee at the booked late rate for each one back; maintenance for a unit only; written once, its bill line given once (a line of the item's service item on a sale to the hirer); never deleted.
Added by: 1791968000000.

#### `rental_charges`
Purpose: damage, missing, cleaning, fuel and other charges at a return, and a cancellation charge.
Key columns: `booking_id`, `return_id`, `booking_line_id`, `kind` (damage, missing, cleaning, fuel, other, cancellation), `amount` (> 0), `note`, `line_id`, `created_by`, `created_at`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `booking_id`) -> `rental_bookings`; (`business_id`, `booking_id`, `return_id`) -> `rental_returns`; (`business_id`, `booking_id`, `booking_line_id`) -> `rental_booking_lines`; (`business_id`, `line_id`) -> `business_document_lines`.
Rules: CHECK a return exactly for the kinds other than cancellation; a note for damage and other; UNIQUE (business, bill line); index `rental_charges_booking`. Trigger `rental_charges_guard`: a cancellation charge on a quote or reserved booking, the others on a booking out; kept as noted, its bill line given once (a line of the item's service item on a sale to the hirer); never deleted.
Added by: 1791968000000.

#### `rental_booking_events`
Purpose: everything that happened to a booking.
Key columns: `booking_id`, `from_status`, `to_status`, `note`, `created_by`, `created_at`.
Rules: CHECK a status or a note, a from-status only with a to-status; index `rental_booking_events_booking`. Trigger `rental_booking_events_append_only` (function `rental_append_only`).
Added by: 1791968000000.

**What is free** (functions, no table): `rental_holds(business, item)` (the stretches of time a booking holds an item's units or pool: booked, out, back, each with the line's turnaround time), `rental_unit_clash(business, item, unit, from, until, except booking, except line)`, `rental_pool_peak(business, item, from, until, except booking, except line)`, `rental_check_line(business, line)` (locks the item's row, raises a sentence when a unit is held by another booking or a pool would be held beyond what it has), `rental_check_booking(business, booking)` and `rental_time_words(instant, time zone)`.

## Fuel stations

A fuel station's own tables (type `fuel_station`), added by 1791969000000 fuel-station. Module `fuel` ("Tanks, nozzles & shifts", optional, fuel_station only, on) needs catalog, contacts, sales, inventory and purchasing; permissions `fuel.view`, `fuel.create` (one's own shift, the morning dips, tanker loads), `fuel.edit` (any attendant's shift, fleet vehicles), `fuel.approve` (reopening a shift, credit over a limit, a load outside the density tolerance, correcting a dip, a meter change, cancelling a load), `fuel.export`, `fuel.print`. Role template `pump_attendant` (Pump attendant (DSM), fuel_station only): `fuel.view` and `fuel.create`. Number series kind `fuel_shift` (FS/…; not a GST document). How they are used: [BUSINESS_PLATFORM_DATA_MODEL.md › Fuel stations](BUSINESS_PLATFORM_DATA_MODEL.md#fuel-stations); the code is in `backend/src/modules/business/fuel/`. Functions: `fuel_append_only()` (refuses changing or deleting a price, a meter change and a close's rows), `fuel_time_words(instant, time zone)` (an instant in words on the business's clock, for the sentences raised), `fuel_price_at(business, location, fuel, instant)` (the price in force: the latest effective by then), `fuel_nozzle_open_shift(business, nozzle)` (the open shift a nozzle is on, with its number and attendant).

#### `fuel_settings`
Purpose: the tolerances the stock register checks against, the test measure and the bell's warning days.
Key columns: PK `business_id`, `density_tolerance` (0-20, default 3.0), `variation_percent` (0-20, default 4.00), `petrol_loss_percent` (default 0.75), `petrol_loss_above_percent` (0.60), `diesel_loss_percent` (0.25), `diesel_loss_above_percent` (0.20), each 0-5, `above_600_kl`, `test_litres` (0-100, default 5.000), `stamping_warn_days` (1-180, default 30), `updated_by`, `updated_at`. The defaults are used until the row is made (on first save).
Added by: 1791969000000.

#### `fuel_products`
Purpose: each fuel and the goods item it is sold and bought as.
Key columns: `sku_id`, `family` (petrol, diesel, cng), `sort_order`, `active`, `created_by`/`created_at`, `updated_by`/`updated_at`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `sku_id`) -> `catalog_skus`.
Rules: UNIQUE (business, item). Trigger `fuel_products_guard`: the item is a goods item of the business, kept by quantity with no batches, serials or kit, counted in litres (CNG in kilos), on a 0% slab; the fuel keeps its item and family; never deleted.
Added by: 1791969000000.

#### `fuel_tanks`
Purpose: each tank of an outlet and the fuel it holds.
Key columns: `location_id`, `product_id`, `tank_no`, `capacity` (litres, up to 1,000,000), `sort_order`, `active`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `location_id`) -> `business_locations`; (`business_id`, `product_id`) -> `fuel_products`.
Rules: unique index `fuel_tanks_number` (business, outlet, number in any case). Trigger `fuel_tanks_guard`: no CNG; keeps its outlet and fuel; its capacity never below its latest dip; never deleted.
Added by: 1791969000000.

#### `fuel_dispensers`
Purpose: each dispensing unit with its Weights & Measures stamping.
Key columns: `location_id`, `unit_no`, `make`, `serial_no`, `stamped_on`, `stamping_due`, `status` (in_use, suspended, retired), `status_note`, `sort_order`.
Rules: CHECK the due date after the stamping; a note unless in use. Unique index `fuel_dispensers_number` (business, outlet, number in any case). Trigger `fuel_dispensers_guard`: keeps its outlet; out of use only while no shift is open on its nozzles; never deleted.
Added by: 1791969000000.

#### `fuel_nozzles`
Purpose: each nozzle, its tank and its totaliser.
Key columns: `location_id`, `dispenser_id`, `nozzle_no` (capital letters, digits, `/` and `-`, up to 10), `product_id`, `tank_id` (none for CNG), `reading` (>= 0, three places), `sort_order`, `active`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `location_id`, `dispenser_id`) -> `fuel_dispensers`; (`business_id`, `location_id`, `tank_id`) -> `fuel_tanks`; (`business_id`, `product_id`) -> `fuel_products`.
Rules: unique index `fuel_nozzles_number` (business, outlet, number); index `fuel_nozzles_dispenser`. Trigger `fuel_nozzles_guard`: a petrol or diesel nozzle draws from a tank of its fuel, a CNG nozzle from none; it keeps its unit, fuel and tank; it is switched off only while on no open shift; its reading changes only in the moment of a shift's close or reopening or a meter change (`created_at` = now()); never deleted.
Added by: 1791969000000.

#### `fuel_meter_changes`
Purpose: a manager's meter change of a nozzle.
Key columns: `nozzle_id`, `from_reading`, `to_reading` (not the same), `reason`, `created_by`, `created_at`.
Rules: index `fuel_meter_changes_nozzle`. Trigger `fuel_meter_changes_check`: from the nozzle's reading now, while no shift is open on it, when written. Trigger `fuel_meter_changes_append_only` (function `fuel_append_only`).
Added by: 1791969000000.

#### `fuel_measures`
Purpose: the stamped measures of an outlet (the 5-litre measure of the nozzle test).
Key columns: `location_id`, `name`, `litres` (0-100), `serial_no`, `stamped_on`, `stamping_due`, `active`.
Rules: CHECK the due date after the stamping. Trigger `fuel_measures_guard`: keeps its outlet; never deleted.
Added by: 1791969000000.

#### `fuel_prices`
Purpose: each fuel's price at an outlet from the time it takes effect.
Key columns: `location_id`, `product_id`, `price` (> 0, VAT included), `effective_from`, `note`, `request_key`, `request_hash`, `created_by`, `created_at`.
Rules: UNIQUE (business, outlet, fuel, effective from) and (business, request key, outlet, fuel); index `fuel_prices_lookup`. Trigger `fuel_prices_check`: a fuel in use, written when set, taking effect within a week back and a month ahead, never before the latest close that sold the fuel at the outlet. Trigger `fuel_prices_append_only` (function `fuel_append_only`).
Added by: 1791969000000.

#### `fuel_vehicles`
Purpose: a fleet customer's vehicles filled on credit.
Key columns: `contact_id`, `registration` (capital letters and digits, 4-15), `description`, `product_id` (the fuel it takes on credit; none: any), `fill_limit` (> 0), `active`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `contact_id`) -> `business_contacts`; (`business_id`, `product_id`) -> `fuel_products`.
Rules: unique index `fuel_vehicles_registration` (business, registration, while in use); index `fuel_vehicles_contact`. Trigger `fuel_vehicles_customer` (function `business_contact_is_customer`). Trigger `fuel_vehicles_guard`: keeps its customer and registration; never deleted.
Added by: 1791969000000.

#### `fuel_shifts`
Purpose: an attendant's shift at an outlet.
Key columns: `location_id`, `shift_no` (series kind `fuel_shift`), `series_id`, `attendant_id` -> `users`, `attendant_name`, `status` (open, closed), `opened_at`, `notes`, `request_key`, `request_hash`, `created_by`/`created_at`, `updated_by`/`updated_at`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `location_id`) -> `business_locations`; (`business_id`, `series_id`) -> `business_number_series`.
Rules: CHECK opened no later than written. UNIQUE (business, number), (business, request key); unique index `fuel_shifts_one_open` (business, outlet, attendant, while open); indexes `fuel_shifts_period` and `fuel_shifts_open`. Trigger `fuel_shifts_insert_check`: starts open, from readings taken within the last 24 hours, for a member of the business's staff. Trigger `fuel_shifts_guard`: keeps its number, outlet, attendant and opening; closes only with a close in force; reopens only when its close is reopened, before another shift has opened on its nozzles; never deleted.
Added by: 1791969000000.

#### `fuel_shift_nozzles`
Purpose: the nozzles a shift runs, from their opening readings.
Key columns: PK (`business_id`, `shift_id`, `nozzle_id`), `nozzle_no`, `product_id`, `tank_id`, `opening`, `added_by`, `added_at`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `shift_id`) -> `fuel_shifts`; (`business_id`, `nozzle_id`) -> `fuel_nozzles`.
Rules: index `fuel_shift_nozzles_nozzle`. Trigger `fuel_shift_nozzles_check`: onto an open shift of the nozzle's outlet, the nozzle switched on and its unit in use, on no other open shift, opening from its reading, after the last close on it; keeps its opening; stays on a shift it was closed on.
Added by: 1791969000000.

#### `fuel_shift_closes`
Purpose: each close of a shift, and a manager's reopening of it.
Key columns: `shift_id`, `seq` (1-100), `closed_at`, `expected`, `credit`, `walk_in`, `cash`, `other`, `difference`, `note`, `document_id` (the walk-in fuel bill), `walk_in_key`, `approved_by`, `request_key`, `request_hash`, `closed_by`, `created_at`, `reopened_at`, `reopened_by`, `reopen_reason`, `reopen_key`, `reopen_hash`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `shift_id`) -> `fuel_shifts`; (`business_id`, `document_id`) -> `business_documents`.
Rules: CHECK `expected` = `credit` + `walk_in`; `other` <= `walk_in`; `difference` = `cash` + `other` − `walk_in`; closed no later than written; the reopening's columns together. UNIQUE (business, shift, seq), (business, request key), (business, walk-in key), (business, bill), (business, reopen key); unique index `fuel_shift_closes_current` (one close in force a shift); index `fuel_shift_closes_period`. Trigger `fuel_shift_closes_check`: of an open shift, when written, from readings after its opening, numbered in turn; keeps its figures and its bill; reopened when written, and then no longer changes; never deleted. Constraint trigger `fuel_shift_closes_adds_up` (deferred; function `fuel_close_adds_up`): a closing reading for every nozzle of the shift; the fuels' litres are the nozzles' and their credit litres and money the slips'; each at the price in force through the shift (a price changed while it was open raises a sentence); the close adds up to its fuels and takings; the walk-in sales are on a walk-in fuel bill to no one by name, paid by the takings and then the cash; no takings without walk-in sales.
Added by: 1791969000000.

#### `fuel_close_readings`
Purpose: each nozzle's readings at a close.
Key columns: PK (`business_id`, `close_id`, `nozzle_id`), `shift_id`, `product_id`, `tank_id`, `opening`, `closing`, `test`, `litres`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `shift_id`, `close_id`) -> `fuel_shift_closes`; (`business_id`, `shift_id`, `nozzle_id`) -> `fuel_shift_nozzles`.
Rules: CHECK `closing` >= `opening`, at most 1,000,000 apart; `litres` = `closing` − `opening` − `test` (>= 0). Indexes `fuel_close_readings_nozzle` and `fuel_close_readings_tank`. Trigger `fuel_close_readings_check`: from the shift's opening of the nozzle, written with the close. Trigger `fuel_close_readings_append_only`.
Added by: 1791969000000.

#### `fuel_close_products`
Purpose: each fuel a close sold, on credit and to walk-in buyers.
Key columns: PK (`business_id`, `close_id`, `product_id`), `shift_id`, `price`, `litres`, `credit_litres`, `walk_in_litres`, `credit_amount`, `walk_in_amount`, `amount`, `line_id`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `line_id`) -> `business_document_lines`.
Rules: CHECK `walk_in_litres` = `litres` − `credit_litres`; `walk_in_amount` = round(`walk_in_litres` × `price`, 2); `amount` = `credit_amount` + `walk_in_amount`; a bill line exactly when some went to walk-in buyers. UNIQUE (business, bill line); index `fuel_close_products_product`. Trigger `fuel_close_products_check`: written with the close; the line is the walk-in bill's, of the fuel's item, for its litres and money. Trigger `fuel_close_products_append_only`.
Added by: 1791969000000.

#### `fuel_credit_slips`
Purpose: each credit fill of a fleet vehicle at a close.
Key columns: `close_id`, `shift_id`, `seq` (1-500), `contact_id`, `vehicle_id`, `registration`, `product_id`, `litres` (> 0), `price`, `amount`, `slip_no`, `over_limit`, `document_id`, `line_id`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `contact_id`, `vehicle_id`) -> `fuel_vehicles`; (`business_id`, `document_id`) -> `business_documents`; (`business_id`, `line_id`) -> `business_document_lines`.
Rules: CHECK `amount` = round(`litres` × `price`, 2). UNIQUE (business, close, seq), (business, bill line); indexes `fuel_credit_slips_contact` and `fuel_credit_slips_vehicle`. Trigger `fuel_credit_slips_check`: written with the close; a vehicle of the customer in use, the fuel it takes, within its fill limit unless the close's approver allowed it; its line its own on the customer's fuel bill, of the fuel's item, for its litres and money. Trigger `fuel_credit_slips_append_only`.
Added by: 1791969000000.

#### `fuel_close_payments`
Purpose: a close's takings other than cash (UPI, cards, fleet cards).
Key columns: PK (`business_id`, `close_id`, `seq`), `method` (upi, card, bank), `method_name`, `amount` (> 0), `reference`, `payment_id`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `payment_id`) -> `business_payments`.
Rules: UNIQUE (business, payment). Trigger `fuel_close_payments_check`: the payment on the walk-in bill as handed in, written with the close. Trigger `fuel_close_payments_append_only`.
Added by: 1791969000000.

#### `fuel_dips`
Purpose: each tank's morning dip.
Key columns: `location_id`, `tank_id`, `dip_on`, `taken_at`, `dip_cm`, `stock` (litres), `water_cm`, `density` (600-1,100), `temperature` (−20 to 70), `note`, `request_key`, `request_hash`, `created_by`/`created_at`, `corrected_by`, `corrected_at`, `correction_reason`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `location_id`, `tank_id`) -> `fuel_tanks`.
Rules: CHECK a correction's columns together. UNIQUE (business, tank, day), (business, request key); index `fuel_dips_location`. Trigger `fuel_dips_check`: of a tank in use, written when typed, within a week of its day, taken on that day by the business's clock and no later than now, at most the tank's capacity; keeps its tank and day; changes only as a correction made now with the reason; never deleted.
Added by: 1791969000000.

#### `fuel_receipts`
Purpose: each tanker load: accepted (a purchase), refused, or cancelled by a manager.
Key columns: `location_id`, `contact_id`, `supplier_name`, `invoice_no`, `invoice_date`, `lorry_no` (capital letters and digits), `received_at`, `seals_ok`, `status` (accepted, refused, cancelled), `refuse_reason`, `density_approved_by`, `density_note`, `document_id` (the purchase), `notes`, `request_key`, `request_hash`, `created_by`/`created_at`, `cancelled_at`, `cancelled_by`, `cancel_reason`, `cancel_key`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `location_id`) -> `business_locations`; (`business_id`, `contact_id`) -> `business_contacts`; (`business_id`, `document_id`) -> `business_documents`.
Rules: CHECK a reason exactly when refused, a purchase unless refused, the cancellation's columns exactly when cancelled, a manager's density acceptance with its note and never on a refused load, decanted no later than written. UNIQUE (business, request key), (business, purchase), (business, cancel key); index `fuel_receipts_period`. Trigger `fuel_receipts_supplier` (function `business_contact_is_supplier`). Trigger `fuel_receipts_check`: accepted or refused when written, within a week of its decanting; an accepted load's purchase is from its supplier at the outlet under the invoice's number; only an accepted load is cancelled, and a refused or cancelled one no longer changes; never deleted.
Added by: 1791969000000.

#### `fuel_receipt_lines`
Purpose: each tank a load went into, with the density check and the dips.
Key columns: PK (`business_id`, `receipt_id`, `seq`), `tank_id`, `product_id`, `invoice_litres` (> 0), `invoice_amount`, `invoice_density`, `receipt_density` (600-1,100), `tolerance`, `dip_before`, `dip_after`, `decant_density`, `decant_by`, `decant_at`, `line_id`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `receipt_id`) -> `fuel_receipts`; (`business_id`, `tank_id`) -> `fuel_tanks`; (`business_id`, `line_id`) -> `business_document_lines`.
Rules: CHECK both dips or neither, the dip after no less than the one before; the decant density with who and when. UNIQUE (business, purchase line); unique index `fuel_receipt_lines_tank` (a tank once a load); index `fuel_receipt_lines_tank_time`. Trigger `fuel_receipt_lines_check`: written with the load, of a tank in use at the outlet for its fuel; a refused load neither decanted nor bought; an accepted load's tanks dipped before and after, at most their capacity, its density within the tolerance unless a manager accepted it, each a line of its purchase of the fuel's item for the invoice's litres and value; the decant density written once; never deleted.
Added by: 1791969000000.

## Hotels, lodges & guest houses

A hotel's, lodge's or guest house's own tables (type `lodging`), added by 1791970000000 lodging. Module `lodging` ("Rooms & stays", optional, lodging only, on) needs catalog, contacts and sales; permissions `lodging.view`, `lodging.create` (reservations, check-in and check-out, charges, the night audit, Form-III), `lodging.edit` (room status), `lodging.approve` (a guest in the house's rate, voiding a charge, a guest's bill left due at check-out), `lodging.cancel` (cancellations and no-shows), `lodging.refund` (advances and deposits handed back), `lodging.print` (registration cards and the guest register). Role templates `front_desk` (Front desk, lodging only) and `housekeeping` (Housekeeping, lodging only). Number series kind `stay` (ST/…; not a GST document); collection refunds' `source` `lodging`. How they are used: [BUSINESS_PLATFORM_DATA_MODEL.md › Hotels, lodges & guest houses](BUSINESS_PLATFORM_DATA_MODEL.md#hotels-lodges--guest-houses); the code is in `backend/src/modules/business/lodging/`. Functions: `lodging_append_only()` (refuses changing or deleting a stay's history, a room's status log and a stay's receipts), `lodging_today(business)` (the business's date in its time zone), `lodging_day_words(day)` (a date in words, for the sentences raised), `lodging_check_type(business, stay)` (locks the stay's room type and raises a sentence when its reservations and stays on a night would outnumber its rooms in use at the location).

#### `lodging_settings`
Purpose: the two GST slabs of a room night and the limit between them, the weekend nights and the registration card's terms.
Key columns: PK `business_id`, `low_tax_rate_id` and `high_tax_rate_id` -> `business_tax_rates`, `slab_limit` (> 0, default 7500.00), `weekend_nights` (SMALLINT[] of 1-7, default {5,6}), `terms` (up to 2,000), `updated_by`, `updated_at`.
Rules: CHECK both slabs or neither, and two different ones. Trigger `lodging_settings_check`: both slabs in use, the lower's rate below the higher's.
Added by: 1791970000000.

#### `lodging_room_types`
Purpose: each kind of room, its occupancy and its rates.
Key columns: `sku_id`, `base_occupancy` (1-20), `max_occupancy` (1-30, at least the base), `max_extra_beds` (0-5), `rack_rate` (> 0), `weekend_rate` (> 0), `extra_adult_rate`, `child_rate`, `extra_bed_rate` (each >= 0; the bed rate required with extra beds), `description`, `sort_order`, `active`, `created_by`/`created_at`, `updated_by`/`updated_at`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `sku_id`) -> `catalog_skus`.
Rules: UNIQUE (business, item); index `lodging_room_types_list`. Trigger `lodging_room_types_guard`: the item is a service of the business counted in nights, not a kit; the type keeps its item; it is switched off only when no stay of it is reserved or in the house; never deleted.
Added by: 1791970000000.

#### `lodging_rooms`
Purpose: each room of a property and its housekeeping status.
Key columns: `location_id`, `room_type_id`, `room_no` (letters, digits, spaces, `/` and `-`, up to 20), `floor`, `status` (vacant_clean, vacant_dirty, occupied, out_of_order), `status_note` (required out of order), `status_by`, `status_at`, `sort_order`, `active`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `location_id`) -> `business_locations`; (`business_id`, `room_type_id`) -> `lodging_room_types`.
Rules: unique index `lodging_rooms_number` (business, location, number in any case); index `lodging_rooms_type`. Trigger `lodging_rooms_guard`: starts vacant, on a room type in use; keeps its location; occupied only while a guest is checked in to it, and not left while they are; its type changes and it goes out of use only while no stay is reserved for it or in it and its type's stays from today still fit in the rooms left; never deleted. Trigger `lodging_rooms_log` (after the row) writes each status to `lodging_room_status_log`.
Added by: 1791970000000.

#### `lodging_room_status_log`
Purpose: every status a room took.
Key columns: `room_id`, `from_status`, `to_status`, `note`, `created_by`, `created_at`.
Rules: index `lodging_room_status_log_room`. Trigger `lodging_room_status_log_append_only` (function `lodging_append_only`).
Added by: 1791970000000.

#### `lodging_stays`
Purpose: a reservation and the stay it becomes.
Key columns: `location_id`, `stay_no` (series kind `stay`), `series_id`, `contact_id`, `guest_name`, `guest_phone`, `bill_to_contact_id`, `source` (walk_in, phone, ota), `ota_name`, `ota_booking_id`, `status` (reserved, in_house, checked_out, cancelled, no_show), `arrival`, `departure` (DATE), `room_type_id`, `room_id`, `adults` (1-30), `children` (0-30), `extra_beds` (0-5), `base_occupancy`, `rack_rate`, `weekend_rate`, `extra_adult_rate`, `child_rate`, `extra_bed_rate`, `agreed_rate`, `estimate_subtotal`, `estimate_tax`, `estimate_total`, `arrived_from`, `going_to`, `purpose`, `notes`, `checked_in_at`, `checked_in_by`, `checkin_key`, `checkin_hash`, `checked_out_at`, `checked_out_by`, `checkout_key`, `checkout_hash`, `document_id`, `cancelled_at`, `cancelled_by`, `cancel_reason`, `cancel_key`, `cancel_hash`, `request_key`, `request_hash`, `created_by`/`created_at`, `updated_by`/`updated_at`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `location_id`) -> `business_locations`; (`business_id`, `series_id`) -> `business_number_series`; (`business_id`, `contact_id`) and (`business_id`, `bill_to_contact_id`) -> `business_contacts`; (`business_id`, `room_type_id`) -> `lodging_room_types`; (`business_id`, `room_id`) -> `lodging_rooms`; (`business_id`, `document_id`) -> `business_documents`.
Rules: CHECK the departure after the arrival, at most 180 nights; an OTA's name and booking id exactly for an OTA; the company billed not the guest; adults beyond the base only with an extra-adult rate, extra beds only with a bed rate; `estimate_total` = `estimate_subtotal` + `estimate_tax`; the check-in's, check-out's and cancellation's columns together and exactly with their status; a room once checked in; a bill when checked out, and only when checked out, cancelled or a no-show. UNIQUE (business, number), (business, request key), (business, bill), (business, check-in key), (business, check-out key), (business, cancel key). Exclusion constraint `lodging_stays_room_free` (GiST: `int8range(room_id, room_id, '[]')` and `daterange(arrival, departure)` overlapping, while a room is given and the stay is reserved or in the house): a room is held by one stay a night. Indexes `lodging_stays_period`, `lodging_stays_status`, `lodging_stays_contact`, `lodging_stays_type` (reserved or in the house), `lodging_stays_room`. Trigger `lodging_stays_customer` (function `business_contact_is_customer`). Trigger `lodging_stays_bill_to`: the company billed is a customer. Trigger `lodging_stays_insert_check`: starts reserved, arriving today or later on the business's clock, written when saved. Trigger `lodging_stays_guard`: keeps its number, guest and location; reserved → in_house, cancelled or no_show (once the arrival day has come), in_house → checked_out; a room in use, at its location and of its type, vacant and clean to check in or move to; checked in from the arrival day to the night before the departure; the arrival changes only while reserved, never to a day passed; a guest in the house keeps a room, the adults and children, and a departure after the last night posted and from today; checked out on the business's date (one night at least); the check-in, check-out and cancellation written once, with their step; billed once, on a sale to the guest or the company billed; an ended stay never changes; never deleted. Constraint trigger `lodging_stays_type_free` (deferred; function `lodging_check_type`): a room type's reservations and stays on a night never outnumber its rooms in use at the location. Constraint trigger `lodging_stays_done` (deferred): a stay checked in has its adults and children recorded as occupants; a stay checked out has every night billed, every charge billed or voided, and its occupants gone.
Added by: 1791970000000; 1791977000000 (`lodging_stays_guard` lets an erasure's change through: `business_privacy_erasing`).

#### `lodging_stay_guests`
Purpose: every occupant of a stay, with their photo ID and Form-III reports.
Key columns: `stay_id`, `seq` (1-60), `full_name`, `child`, `gender` (female, male, other), `age` (0-120), `phone`, `address`, `nationality`, `foreigner`, `id_kind` (aadhaar, voter_id, driving_licence, passport, pan_card, other), `id_ref` (capital letters, digits, `/` and `-`), `visa_type`, `visa_no`, `arrived_india_on`, `next_destination`, `arrived_at`, `departed_at`, `frro_arrival_ack`, `frro_arrival_at`, `frro_arrival_by`, `frro_departure_ack`, `frro_departure_at`, `frro_departure_by`, `created_by`/`created_at`, `updated_by`/`updated_at`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `stay_id`) -> `lodging_stays`.
Rules: CHECK the ID's kind and number together; an Aadhaar's number exactly four digits; an adult has a photo ID; a foreigner has their passport as it, a visa (or OCI card) type and number, the date they arrived in India and the next destination, and only a foreigner has them or Form-III marks; each mark with who and when; the departure's only after they left. UNIQUE (business, stay, seq); indexes `lodging_stay_guests_stay` and `lodging_stay_guests_foreign`. Trigger `lodging_stay_guests_guard`: recorded with a stay in the house, when they arrive; keeps its stay, place and arrival; details change only while the stay is in the house; leaves with the check-out; each Form-III mark written once; never deleted.
Added by: 1791970000000.

#### `lodging_receipts`
Purpose: the advances and deposits taken for a stay.
Key columns: PK (`business_id`, `collection_id`), `stay_id`, `purpose` (advance, deposit), `created_by`, `created_at`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `collection_id`) -> `business_collections`; (`business_id`, `stay_id`) -> `lodging_stays`.
Rules: index `lodging_receipts_stay`. Trigger `lodging_receipts_check`: a stay reserved or in the house, a new receipt in its guest's name by cash, card, UPI or bank. Trigger `lodging_receipts_append_only`.
Added by: 1791970000000.

#### `lodging_charges`
Purpose: a stay's folio: room nights, extras, and a cancellation or no-show charge.
Key columns: `stay_id`, `kind` (room_night, extra, cancellation), `night` (DATE), `room_id`, `sku_id`, `label`, `quantity` (> 0, up to 10,000), `rate_kind` (rack, weekend, agreed), `room_rate`, `extra_adults`, `extra_adult_rate`, `children`, `child_rate`, `extra_beds`, `extra_bed_rate`, `unit_price`, `amount`, `tax_mode`, `tax_rate_id`, `tax_rate`, `net`, `tax`, `note`, `line_id`, `void_reason`, `voided_by`, `voided_at`, `request_key`, `created_by`, `created_at`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `stay_id`) -> `lodging_stays`; (`business_id`, `room_id`) -> `lodging_rooms`; (`business_id`, `sku_id`) -> `catalog_skus`; (`business_id`, `tax_rate_id`) -> `business_tax_rates`; (`business_id`, `line_id`) -> `business_document_lines`.
Rules: CHECK a room and a rate kind exactly for a room night; a room night is one, its `amount` = `room_rate` + the extra adults, children and beds at their rates, and its price its amount; its value columns only on a room night; a room night and a cancellation charge before GST with a slab; `net` = `amount` before GST (or `net` + `tax` = `amount` including it), `tax` = round(`net` × `tax_rate` / 100, 2) before GST; a void with who, when and why, never on a billed charge; a request key on an extra and a cancellation charge. UNIQUE (business, bill line), (business, request key); unique index `lodging_charges_one_night` (business, stay, night) on room nights not voided; index `lodging_charges_stay`. Trigger `lodging_charges_guard`: posted when written; a cancellation charge on a reservation being cancelled, of its room type's item at a room-night slab; otherwise on a stay in the house: an extra a service item, not a room type's, charged on the day at its item's slab; a room night one of the stay's nights that has come, for its room, of its room type's item, at the stay's rate for the night (agreed, else weekend on the settings' weekend nights, else rack, with the extra adults, children and beds) and the slab its value decides against the limit; keeps its figures; its bill line given once, a line of its own item on a sale to the guest or the company billed; voided once, before it is billed, while the guest is in the house; never deleted.
Added by: 1791970000000.

#### `lodging_stay_events`
Purpose: everything that happened to a stay.
Key columns: `stay_id`, `from_status`, `to_status`, `note`, `created_by`, `created_at`.
Rules: CHECK a status or a note; a from-status only with a to-status. Index `lodging_stay_events_stay`. Trigger `lodging_stay_events_append_only` (function `lodging_append_only`).
Added by: 1791970000000.

## Tasks, issues & projects

Linked sections: [data model](BUSINESS_PLATFORM_DATA_MODEL.md#tasks-issues--projects) and [ARCHITECTURE.md §4.3](ARCHITECTURE.md#43-tasks-issues--projects-one-model-for-both-worlds). Migration `1791620000000_tasks-issues-projects.sql`; module code `backend/src/modules/tasks/`.

**One data model for every business, keyed on `business_id`.** A restaurant uses it through its own business row (`businesses.legacy_restaurant_id`, `restaurant_business_id()`), so the restaurant and every other type share the same tables, reports and template library. Only *where* and *who* differ: a restaurant names an `outlet_id`, a restaurant `employee_id` (staff with or without a login) and a restaurant role (`role_id`); every other type names a `location_id`, a `business_resources` row (`resource_id`) and a `business_roles` row (`business_role_id`). Users are the shared `users` table in both. When the restaurant moves onto memberships and locations (plans D and E, [ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md) §9.3), the restaurant-only columns fall out of use and nothing else changes.

Modules (all optional, feature group "Operations" on the restaurant's Features screen): `tasks`; `issues` and `projects`, which need `tasks`. A restaurant starts with all three. For every other type they are off by default and the owner switches them on; `tasks` and `issues` are recommended for a supermarket and a pharmacy, and `tasks` for healthcare. Permissions: `tasks.view / create / assign / review / manage`, `issues.view / create / assign / review / manage` and `projects.view / create / manage`, in `business_permissions` (modules `tasks`, `issues` and `projects`) and in the restaurant `permissions` table (module `tasks`).

Every table but `platform_task_categories` carries `business_id NOT NULL`, and every one but `business_project_members`, `business_task_assignees` and `business_task_history` also carries `UNIQUE (business_id, id)`; references inside the module, and to `business_locations`, `business_resources` and `business_roles`, are composite `(business_id, …)` keys. Each table below was added by 1791620000000_tasks-issues-projects.

**Trigger `business_task_world_check`** (on tasks, schedules, issues, projects, members and assignees) refuses with a sentence (SQL state 23514): an outlet that is not the business's own restaurant's, a location on a restaurant business, a restaurant employee or role of another restaurant (system roles allowed), and a user (`user_id`, `assignee_user_id`, `owner_user_id`) who is neither a member of the business nor a user of its restaurant.

Every change writes `business_task_history` and `business_audit`. Assigning a task, and reporting or assigning an issue, also write `business_events` (`task.assigned`, `issue.reported`, `issue.assigned`) for a later WhatsApp / SMS publisher.

#### `platform_task_categories`
Purpose: the categories every business shares: Stock check, Audit, Cleaning, Day to day task, Hiring, Maintenance & repair work, Marketing, Opening & closing, Order placement & procurement, Production, Project management, Food safety & hygiene, Health & safety, Others.
Key columns: `code` (TEXT PK, `^[a-z_]{2,40}$`), `name`, `icon`, `sort_order`.
Relations: referenced by `category_code` on templates, schedules, tasks and issues.
Added by: 1791620000000, which also creates its rows.

#### `business_task_templates`
Purpose: a business's own checklist template (the built-in ones are code, `modules/tasks/library/`).
Key columns: `name` (not blank), `description`, `category_code`, `priority` (low/normal/high/urgent), `requires_review`, `estimated_minutes` (1–1440), `library_code` (the built-in template it was copied from), `active`, `created_by`, `updated_by`.
Rules: index `business_task_templates_list` (business, active, name).
Added by: 1791620000000.

#### `business_task_template_items`
Purpose: one checklist line of a template. A task copies them into `business_task_items`, so editing a template never changes a checklist already done.
Key columns: `template_id`, `position` (0–500), `kind` (section, checkbox, yes_no, number, text, photo, signature, choice), `label` (not blank), `help`, `required`, `unit`, `min_value`, `max_value`, `options` (jsonb `[{label, fail}]`), `expected` (yes/no: the answer that passes), `photo_required`, `critical` (a failed critical item fails the whole checklist, whatever its score), `raise_issue`, `fail_severity` (low/medium/high/critical).
Relations: (`business_id`, `template_id`) -> `business_task_templates` (ON DELETE CASCADE).
Rules: CHECK `business_task_template_items_range` (min ≤ max); CHECK `business_task_template_items_choice` (`options` is an array for a choice); index by template and position.
Added by: 1791620000000.

#### `business_task_schedules`
Purpose: a recurring task. Its occurrences are made lazily, each once, up to `generated_through`.
Key columns: `title`, `description`, `template_id` or `library_code`, `category_code`, `priority`, `location_id` / `outlet_id`, `assign_mode` (people, role, on_shift, unassigned), `frequency` (daily, weekdays, weekly, monthly, every_n_days, per_shift), `every_n` (1–365), `weekdays` smallint[] (0 = Sunday), `month_day` (1–31), `due_times` time[] (one occurrence per time; a time before the day's cut-off hour, `day_cutoff_hour` in the restaurant's or the business's settings, belongs to the trading day before), `shift_id` (per_shift at a restaurant: only this shift; NULL = every active shift), `window_minutes` (5–1440: the task opens this long before it is due; NULL = at the start of the day), `starts_on`, `ends_on`, `requires_review`, `active`, `generated_through` (occurrences exist up to this day).
Relations: (`business_id`, `template_id`) -> `business_task_templates`; (`business_id`, `location_id`) -> `business_locations`; `outlet_id` -> `outlets`; `shift_id` -> `shifts`; `category_code` -> `platform_task_categories`.
Rules: CHECKs `business_task_schedules_dates` (end not before start), `_one_place` and `_one_template` (at most one of each pair), `_times` (1 to 12 due times) and `_shape` (the shape fits the frequency: every_n_days needs `every_n`, weekly and weekdays need weekdays, monthly needs `month_day`); trigger `business_task_schedules_world`; partial index `business_task_schedules_active`.
Added by: 1791620000000.

#### `business_tasks`
Purpose: one task: one-off, a recurring occurrence, a project task or the fix of an issue.
Key columns: `title`, `description`, `category_code`, `priority`, `status` (todo, in_progress, done, missed, cancelled), `location_id` / `outlet_id`, `assign_mode`, `opens_at`, `due_at`, `closes_at` (after it, not done = missed), `schedule_id` + `occurrence_date` + `occurrence_slot`, `shift_label`, `template_id` / `library_code`, `project_id`, `project_list_id`, `milestone_id`, `issue_id`, `requires_review`, `review_status` (pending, approved, changes_requested), `reviewed_by` / `reviewed_at` / `review_note`, who and when started / completed / cancelled (with `cancel_reason`), counters `item_count`, `answered_count`, `failed_count`, `score_percent`, `critical_failed`.
Relations: composite FKs to `business_locations`, `business_task_schedules`, `business_task_templates`, `business_projects`, `business_issues`, and to `business_project_lists` and `business_project_milestones` with `ON DELETE SET NULL` on that column only; `outlet_id` -> `outlets`.
Rules: CHECKs `business_tasks_one_place`, `_occurrence` (schedule, date and slot all or none), `_done` (done ⇔ completed_at), `_cancelled` (cancelled ⇔ cancelled_at), `_review` (a review status only when review is needed), `_window` (opens_at ≤ due_at) and `_list_needs_project` (a list or milestone only with a project). Partial unique index `business_tasks_occurrence_once` (business, schedule, day, slot): an occurrence is made once however many screens ask. Indexes `business_tasks_due`, `business_tasks_open` (todo and in progress, by `closes_at`) and `business_tasks_project`. Trigger `business_tasks_world`.
Added by: 1791620000000; 1791974000000 (live updates: `business_tasks_live`).

#### `business_task_assignees`
Purpose: who a task or a schedule is for.
