Key columns: `task_id` or `schedule_id` (exactly one), `kind` (user, employee, resource, role, business_role) with exactly the matching id column: `user_id`, `employee_id` (a restaurant employee), `resource_id`, `role_id` (a restaurant role), `business_role_id`.
Relations: task and schedule ON DELETE CASCADE; `user_id` -> `users`; `employee_id` -> `employees`; `role_id` -> `roles`; (`business_id`, `resource_id`) -> `business_resources`; (`business_id`, `business_role_id`) -> `business_roles`.
Rules: CHECKs `business_task_assignees_owner`, `_one` and `_kind`; unique per owner and person (`business_task_assignees_once`); trigger `business_task_assignees_world`.
Added by: 1791620000000; 1791974000000 (live updates: `business_task_assignees_live`).

#### `business_task_items`
Purpose: a task's own copy of its checklist, with the answers.
Key columns: `task_id`, the template item columns, plus `value_bool`, `value_number`, `value_text`, `value_choice`, `note`, `answered`, `failed`, `answered_by`, `answered_at`, `issue_id` (the issue it raised).
Relations: (`business_id`, `task_id`) -> `business_tasks` (CASCADE); (`business_id`, `issue_id`) -> `business_issues` (`ON DELETE SET NULL (issue_id)`).
Rules: CHECKs `business_task_items_answer` (answered ⇔ answered_at), `_fail` (failed only when answered) and `_range`.
Added by: 1791620000000; 1791974000000 (live updates: `business_task_items_live`).

#### `business_issues`
Purpose: a problem to fix. A failed checklist answer can raise one by itself.
Key columns: `number` (unique per business; shown `ISS-0001`), `title`, `description`, `category_code`, `severity` (low/medium/high/critical), `status` (open, in_progress, resolved, closed), `location_id` / `outlet_id`, `asset_label` (equipment, free text), `assignee_user_id`, `due_at`, `reported_by`, `reported_at`, `resolved_at` / `resolved_by`, `resolution_note`, `closed_at` / `closed_by`, sources `source_task_id`, `source_task_item_id`, `source_logbook_entry_id`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `source_task_id`) -> `business_tasks`; (`business_id`, `source_task_item_id`) -> `business_task_items` (`ON DELETE SET NULL (source_task_item_id)`); `source_logbook_entry_id` -> `logbook_entries` (SET NULL); `outlet_id` -> `outlets`.
Rules: CHECKs `business_issues_one_place`, `_resolved` (resolved or closed ⇔ resolved_at) and `_closed` (closed ⇔ closed_at); one issue per log-book entry (partial unique index `business_issues_one_per_logbook_entry`); indexes `business_issues_list` and `business_issues_assignee` (open and in progress); trigger `business_issues_world`.
Added by: 1791620000000; 1791974000000 (live updates: `business_issues_live`).

#### `business_projects`
Purpose: a bigger job with members, milestones and task lists (a new outlet, a festival sale, the annual stock audit).
Key columns: `name`, `description`, `owner_user_id`, `status` (planning, active, on_hold, completed, cancelled), `start_on`, `end_on`, `location_id` / `outlet_id`, `template_code`, `completed_at`.
Rules: CHECKs `business_projects_dates` (end not before start), `_one_place` and `_completed` (completed ⇔ completed_at); index `business_projects_list`; trigger `business_projects_world`.
Added by: 1791620000000.

#### `business_project_members`
Purpose: who is on a project.
Key columns: PK (`business_id`, `project_id`, `user_id`), `role` (owner / member), `added_at`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `project_id`) -> `business_projects` (CASCADE); `user_id` -> `users`.
Rules: index `business_project_members_user`; trigger `business_project_members_world`.
Added by: 1791620000000.

#### `business_project_milestones`
Purpose: a milestone of a project.
Key columns: `project_id`, `name`, `due_on`, `position`, `done_at` / `done_by`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `project_id`) -> `business_projects` (CASCADE). A task's `milestone_id` is cleared when its milestone is deleted.
Rules: CHECK `business_project_milestones_done` (both done columns or neither).
Added by: 1791620000000.

#### `business_project_lists`
Purpose: a task list inside a project.
Key columns: `project_id`, `name` (varchar 80), `position`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `project_id`) -> `business_projects` (CASCADE). Deleting a list keeps its tasks (FK `ON DELETE SET NULL (project_list_id)` on `business_tasks`).
Added by: 1791620000000.

#### `business_task_comments`
Purpose: a comment on a task, an issue or a project.
Key columns: exactly one of `task_id`, `issue_id`, `project_id`; `body` (not blank, up to 2000), `created_by`, `created_at`.
Relations: each owner ON DELETE CASCADE.
Rules: CHECK `business_task_comments_owner`; a partial index per owner.
Added by: 1791620000000.

#### `business_task_files`
Purpose: a photo, signature or file on a task (or one of its checklist items), an issue or a project. Files live in `tasks/<business>/` under the private upload folder (`PRIVATE_UPLOADS_DIR`, `backend/private_uploads` when it is not set) and are only sent through the API.
Key columns: one owner (`task_id`, `issue_id`, `project_id`), optional `item_id` (needs a task), `kind` (photo, signature, file), `stored_name` (generated, unique, `<32 hex>.jpg|png|webp|pdf`), `file_name`, `mime_type` (JPEG, PNG, WebP, PDF), `size_bytes` (≤ 5 MB), `uploaded_by`, `uploaded_at`, `removed_at` / `removed_by` (removing keeps the file).
Relations: each owner and the item ON DELETE CASCADE.
Rules: CHECKs `business_task_files_owner`, `_item` and `_removed` (both removal columns or neither); partial indexes by task and by issue.
Added by: 1791620000000.

#### `business_task_history`
Purpose: the trail on a task, issue or project.
Key columns: one owner (`task_id`, `issue_id`, `project_id`), `action`, `details` (jsonb), `actor_id` (NULL = the system, e.g. "missed"), `created_at`.
Relations: each owner ON DELETE CASCADE, so the trail goes with its task, issue or project.
Rules: **append-only**: trigger `business_task_history_no_update` (function `business_task_history_append_only`) refuses every UPDATE; CHECK `business_task_history_owner`; a partial index per owner.
Added by: 1791620000000.

## GST rates by date

Linked sections: [data model](BUSINESS_PLATFORM_DATA_MODEL.md#gst-rates-by-date) and [ARCHITECTURE.md §4.5](ARCHITECTURE.md#45-gst-rates-by-date-a-change-that-takes-effect-with-no-job-runner). Migration `1791973000000_gst-rates-by-date.sql`; module code `backend/src/modules/tax-changes/`.

**One data model for both engines, keyed on `business_id`.** A GST rate change moves items to another GST rate from a date: a shop's catalogue items (`sku_id`) to one of its slabs (`business_tax_rates`), a restaurant's dishes (`product_id`) to one of its tax rates (`tax_rates`), through its own business row (`businesses.legacy_restaurant_id`, `restaurant_business_id()`). An item's rate is still read from the item (`catalog_skus.tax_rate`, `tax_rate_id`; `products.tax_percentage`, `tax_rate_id`): the functions below move it there on the change's date, and a posted bill keeps the rate its lines were posted at.

**How a change takes effect, with no job runner.** `business_tax_change_apply(business)` applies, in date order, every scheduled item of a shop whose date has come on the business's clock (`now() AT TIME ZONE businesses.timezone`), under the business row's lock, the lock every posting takes first. The server calls it when a request opens a business (the access statement says whether one is due, `tax_changes_due`) and inside every posting. `business_tax_change_apply_menu(restaurant, day)` does the same for dishes due on `day` (NULL: the restaurant's trading date), under the menu's advisory lock (915001, the lock of the menu's imports and price revisions); the server calls it before the menu screens, the guests' menus and a new pre-order's prices, and for an order's own business day before its dishes are priced. Each item moves in a savepoint of its own: it takes the new rate and, when the change keeps the price before GST, the price that keeps it (a shop's member price and branch prices too; a dish's sizes too), its row records what it was and became, and the activity log gets a row in the name of whoever entered the change (`business_audit` `catalog.tax_changed`; a dish's `audit_logs` row like an edit, with `details.via` "GST rate change of 1 Apr 2027: <reason>"). One the database refuses (a check, 23514) is kept as `refused` with the sentence, and the rest move. A kit's own rate is worked out again from its parts.

Functions:
- `restaurant_trading_date(restaurant)`: the main counter's open business day, else the restaurant's date less its morning cut-off (as `tradingDay.js`).
- `business_tax_change_today(business)`: the date a change is measured against: a shop's date in its time zone, a restaurant's trading date.
- `business_tax_change_price(price, from_rate, to_rate)`: a price that includes GST keeping its part before GST at a new rate, to the paisa, half a paisa up (₹118 at 18% is ₹105 at 5%), as the API's preview works it out.
- `business_tax_change_apply(business)` and `business_tax_change_apply_menu(restaurant, day)`: above; each returns the ids of the items it moved.

#### `business_tax_changes`
Purpose: one GST rate change: the rate items move to, from which date, and why. Kept as it was entered.
Key columns: `starts_on` (the first day of the new rate, on the business's or restaurant's own date), `tax_rate_id` (a shop's slab) or `restaurant_tax_rate_id` -> `tax_rates` (a restaurant's rate), `rate` (its percentage when entered), `keeps` (`total`: a price that includes GST keeps what the customer pays; `net`: it keeps its part before GST and the price moves), `reason` (not blank, up to 300), `request_key` + `request_hash` (the request that scheduled it), `created_by`, `created_at`.
Relations: `business_id` -> `businesses`; (`business_id`, `tax_rate_id`) -> `business_tax_rates`; `created_by` -> `users`.
Rules: CHECK exactly one of `tax_rate_id` and `restaurant_tax_rate_id`; UNIQUE (`business_id`, `id`), (`business_id`, `id`, `starts_on`) (the items' key) and (`business_id`, `request_key`); index `business_tax_changes_list`. Trigger `business_tax_changes_world` (function `business_tax_change_world`): a shop's change moves to one of its own slabs, a restaurant's to one of its own tax rates. Trigger `business_tax_changes_kept`: never updated or deleted.
Added by: 1791973000000.

#### `business_tax_change_items`
Purpose: each item a change moves, and what happened to it.
Key columns: `change_id`, `starts_on` (the change's date, here too so what is due is one index away), `sku_id` or `product_id`, `status` (`scheduled`; `applied` on its date; `cancelled` before it; `refused` on its date by the database, with why in `note`), what it was and became when its date came (`from_tax_rate_id` or `from_restaurant_tax_rate_id`, `from_rate`, `to_rate`, `price_before`, `price_after`, `sizes`: a dish's sizes whose price moved, `[{ id, name, before, after }]` as differences from the dish's price), `settled_at` (when it was applied or refused), `note` (why it was refused, or what to put right after it moved), `cancelled_by`, `cancelled_at`, `cancel_reason`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `change_id`, `starts_on`) -> `business_tax_changes`; (`business_id`, `sku_id`) -> `catalog_skus`; `product_id` -> `products`; (`business_id`, `from_tax_rate_id`) -> `business_tax_rates`; `from_restaurant_tax_rate_id` -> `tax_rates`; `cancelled_by` -> `users`. A dish an item names is never deleted (the menu archives it instead).
Rules: CHECKs: exactly one of `sku_id` and `product_id`; applied or refused ⇔ settled with both rates; applied ⇔ both prices; refused needs a note; cancelled ⇔ who, when and a reason. Partial unique indexes `business_tax_change_items_one_sku_a_day` and `_one_dish_a_day`: an item has one scheduled move a day. Partial index `business_tax_change_items_due` (business, date, while scheduled), and indexes by change, item and dish. Trigger `business_tax_change_items_world` (function `business_tax_change_world`): a shop's change moves its catalogue items, a restaurant's its own dishes. Trigger `business_tax_change_items_kept` (function `business_tax_change_items_kept`): never deleted; only a scheduled item changes, once, to applied, refused or cancelled, and what it moves and from when never change; a cancel from its date on (`business_tax_change_today`) is refused: it is in force and kept for good.
Added by: 1791973000000.

## Live updates

Linked section: [ARCHITECTURE.md §3.5](ARCHITECTURE.md#35-live-updates); the stream routes are in [API.md › Live updates](API.md#live-updates). Migrations `1791974000000_live-updates.sql` and `1791976000000_clinic-lab-workspaces.sql` (the lab's); module code `backend/src/modules/live/`. No tables: a trigger on each table a live screen reads calls `pg_notify`, which PostgreSQL delivers to every listening connection only when the transaction commits, in commit order. A change that rolls back, or a savepoint rolled back, is never announced, and identical notifications of one transaction reach the listeners once. Each API process listens on one connection of its own (`application_name` "live updates").

**Channel `live_change`**, what changed: compact JSON with the restaurant (and `outlet`) or the business (and `location`), the `area` and the row's `id`, e.g. `{"restaurant":12,"outlet":3,"area":"order","id":456}` or `{"business":7,"location":2,"area":"sale","id":1001}`; what is not known is left out. Never the row's data.

**Channel `live_session`**, what may end a stream: `{"user": 5}`, `{"restaurant": 2}`, `{"business": 7}` or `{"user": 5, "business": 7}`.

Functions:
- `live_announce(scope, tenant, branch, area, ref)`: sends one `live_change` notification; `scope` is `restaurant` (the branch is an outlet) or `business` (a location).
- `live_announce_business(business, place, outlet, area, ref)`: a business's change, or, for a business that is a restaurant's own (`legacy_restaurant_id`), that restaurant's, with the row's outlet.
- `live_restaurant_row()` (trigger, arguments: the area and the column holding the id): a restaurant row with its own `restaurant_id` and, when it has one, `outlet_id`. An update that changes nothing but `updated_at` is no change; a row moved to another outlet is announced at both.
- `live_restaurant_part()` (trigger, arguments: the parent's area and the column pointing at it): a row announced as its parent (`orders`, `kots`, `bills` or `reservations`), with the parent's restaurant, outlet and id; moved from one parent to another, for both. A parent removed in the same statement announced itself.
- `live_business_row()` (trigger, arguments: the area and the column holding the id): a business row with its `business_id` and, when it has them, `location_id` and `outlet_id`.
- `live_lab_order_part()` (trigger): a lab sample or result announced as its order (`lab`, the order's id), at the order's location. An update that changes nothing but `updated_at` is no change.
- `live_business_event()` (trigger): an event of the outbox, in the area its `kind` starts with (`sale.posted` is `sale`), for its `entity_id`, at the location its payload's `locationId` names.
- `live_session()` (trigger, arguments: pairs of a key and the column holding its id): sends a `live_session` notification.

Triggers (all `AFTER … FOR EACH ROW`):

| Trigger | Table | Fires on | Announces |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `trg_orders_live` | `orders` | insert, update, delete | `order`, the order |
| `trg_order_items_live`, `trg_order_joined_tables_live` | `order_items`, `order_joined_tables` | insert, update, delete | `order`, the order they belong to |
| `trg_kots_live` | `kots` | insert, update, delete | `kot`, the ticket |
| `trg_kot_items_live` | `kot_items` | insert, update, delete | `kot`, the ticket it belongs to |
| `trg_kot_change_slips_live` | `kot_change_slips` | insert, update, delete | `kot_slip` |
| `trg_table_requests_live` | `table_requests` | insert, update, delete | `table_request` |
| `trg_dining_tables_live` | `dining_tables` | insert, update, delete | `table` |
| `trg_bills_live` | `bills` | insert, update, delete | `bill` |
| `trg_payments_live`, `trg_refunds_live` | `payments`, `refunds` | insert, update, delete | `bill`, the bill they belong to |
| `trg_reservations_live` | `reservations` | insert, update, delete | `reservation` |
| `trg_reservation_tables_live` | `reservation_tables` | insert, update, delete | `reservation`, the reservation it belongs to |
| `trg_waitlist_entries_live` | `waitlist_entries` | insert, update, delete | `waitlist` |
| `trg_menu_sold_out_live` | `menu_sold_out` | insert, update, delete | `sold_out` |
| `trg_attendance_punches_live`, `trg_attendance_live` | `attendance_punches`, `attendance` | insert, update, delete | `timeclock` |
| `trg_restaurant_preorders_live` | `restaurant_preorders` | insert, update, delete | `preorder` |
| `trg_business_days_live` | `business_days` | insert, update, delete | `business_day` |
| `business_events_live` | `business_events` | insert | the kind's area (`live_business_event()`) |
| `health_tokens_live` | `health_tokens` | insert, update, delete | `token` |
| `lab_orders_live` | `lab_orders` | insert, update, delete | `lab`, the order |
| `lab_samples_live`, `lab_results_live` | `lab_samples`, `lab_results` | insert, update, delete | `lab`, the order they belong to (`live_lab_order_part()`) |
| `business_appointments_live` | `business_appointments` | insert, update, delete | `appointment` |
| `salon_walk_ins_live` | `salon_walk_ins` | insert, update, delete | `walk_in` |
| `business_tasks_live`, `business_task_assignees_live`, `business_task_items_live` | `business_tasks`, `business_task_assignees`, `business_task_items` | insert, update, delete | `task`, the task |
| `business_issues_live` | `business_issues` | insert, update, delete | `issue` |
| `jewellery_rate_sheets_live` | `jewellery_rate_sheets` | insert | `jewellery_rates` |
| `trg_users_live_session` | `users` | update of `token_version`, `status`, `restaurant_id`, `outlet_id` or `password_must_change` (when one changes) | `live_session` `{user}` |
| `trg_restaurants_live_session` | `restaurants` | update of `status` (when it changes) | `live_session` `{restaurant}` |
| `businesses_live_session` | `businesses` | update of `status` (when it changes) | `live_session` `{business}` |
| `business_memberships_live_session` | `business_memberships` | update of `active`, `role_id` or `location_id` (when one changes) | `live_session` `{user, business}` |
| `business_memberships_live_session_gone` | `business_memberships` | delete | `live_session` `{user, business}` |
| `business_roles_live_session` | `business_roles` | update of `active` (when it changes) | `live_session` `{business}` |

1791974000000's Down half drops its 34 triggers and 7 functions; 1791976000000's drops the lab's three triggers and `live_lab_order_part()`.

## Personal data

Linked sections: [data model](BUSINESS_PLATFORM_DATA_MODEL.md#personal-data), [ARCHITECTURE.md §4.6](ARCHITECTURE.md#46-personal-data-one-model-for-both-engines), [BUSINESS_RULES.md › Personal data](BUSINESS_RULES.md#personal-data) and [API.md › Personal data](API.md#personal-data). Migration `1791977000000_personal-data.sql`; module code `backend/src/modules/personal-data/`.

**One data model for both engines, keyed on `business_id`.** A person is a shop's customer contact (`contact_id`, with the `business_id` in the foreign key) or a restaurant's guest (`customer_id`, the restaurant reached through its business row, `businesses.legacy_restaurant_id`, made by `restaurant_business_id()` when first needed), never both (`num_nonnulls(contact_id, customer_id) = 1`). A guest's rows move with a guest-book merge to the record that stays; nothing else changes on them.

**Erasure on records kept closed.** A customer whose data is erased keeps a nameless contact (`business_contacts.erased_at`). Fourteen tables' guards keep a closed row as it was; each lets one change through: the erased customer's own copies on the row (the columns the erasure blanks, listed by `personal-data/services/personalDataRegistry.js`) and nothing else. `business_exchange_vouchers_rules`, `business_quotations_guard`, `books_customer_order_rules`, `electronics_repair_jobs_closed`, `electronics_warranties_rules`, `salon_append_only`, `agri_farmers_guard`, `hardware_sites_guard`, `garage_job_cards_guard`, `rental_bookings_guard`, `laundry_orders_guard`, `optical_orders_guard`, `lodging_stays_guard` and `jewellery_schemes_guard` start with that check; the rest of each body is unchanged.

Functions:
- `business_privacy_erasing(tbl, before, after)` (STABLE): true when an update of `tbl`'s row (as JSON before and after) changes only that table's erasable columns (and `updated_at`, `updated_by`) and the row's contact is erased. The tables and columns: credit vouchers (`customer_name`, `customer_phone`), quotations (the same), book orders (`customer_name`, `phone`, `notes`), repair jobs (`customer_name`, `customer_phone`, `phone_digits`, `passcode`), warranties (`customer_name`, `customer_phone`, `phone_digits`), salon notes (`body`), farmers (`village`, `relative_name`, `land_acres`, `crops`, `farmer_id`, `notes`), sites (`name`, `address`, `contact_person`, `phone`, `active`), job cards and spectacle orders (`customer_name`, `customer_phone`), rental bookings (`customer_name`, `customer_phone`, `id_kind`, `id_ref`, `licence_no`, `licence_valid_until`, `notes`), garment orders (`customer_name`, `customer_phone`, `notes`), stays (`guest_name`, `guest_phone`, `arrived_from`, `going_to`, `purpose`, `notes`) and gold schemes (`member_name`, `member_phone`, `nominee_name`, `nominee_relation`).
- `business_privacy_person(business, contact, guest)`: refuses a person who is not the business's own (a customer contact of it, or a guest of the restaurant behind it).
- `business_privacy_erased(business, contact, guest)` (STABLE): whether the person's data was erased.
- `business_privacy_today(business)` (STABLE): the business's own date; a restaurant's in its `restaurant_settings.timezone`, any other business's in `businesses.timezone`.

#### `business_privacy_notices`
Purpose: a business's privacy notice, in versions: what is collected and why, how to see, correct or erase it, how to complain and whom to contact, with a short consent notice for each purpose and the privacy contact.
Key columns: `version` (from 1), `notice` (200–8000 characters), `bills_text`, `reminders_text`, `offers_text` (20–600 each), `contact_name`, `contact_phone`, `contact_email`, `published_by`, `published_at`, `request_key` + `request_hash`.
Relations: `business_id` -> `businesses`; `published_by` -> `users`. The target of `business_consents.notice_id` and `table_requests.privacy_notice_id`.
Rules: UNIQUE (`business_id`, `version`), (`business_id`, `id`), (`business_id`, `request_key`); CHECK `business_privacy_notices_contact` (a phone or an e-mail). Trigger `business_privacy_notice_kept` (function of the same name): never changed or deleted; a change is a new version.
Added by: 1791977000000.

#### `business_consents`
Purpose: consent per purpose and per business: given (when, where, how, on which notice version, by whom, recorded by whom) and withdrawn once, with the reason and where. A new consent after a withdrawal is a new row. 1791977000000 made it of the clinic's `health_consents` (1791620004000), whose rows keep their ids, and brought the guest book's consent to offers into it (`customers.marketing_opt_in`: one row per guest who had agreed, channel `import`, given when the guest book says).
Key columns: `contact_id` or `customer_id`, `purpose` (`bills_digital`, `reminders`, `offers`; a clinic patient's `treatment`, `reports_digital`), `notice_id` (the privacy notice shown) and `notice_version` (`v` and its number; a clinic's own consent notice version for a patient's consents given at its desk), `channel` (`counter`, `guests_site`, `phone`, `import`), `method` (`signed_form`, `verbal`, `digital`), `given_by`, `recorded_by` (NULL on the guests' site, and on a consent brought over when the guest book did not say who), `note`, `created_at`, `withdrawn_at`, `withdrawn_by`, `withdrawal_reason`, `withdrawal_channel`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `contact_id`) -> `business_contacts`; `customer_id` -> `customers`; (`business_id`, `notice_id`) -> `business_privacy_notices`; user columns -> `users`.
Rules: CHECKs `business_consents_purpose`, `_method`, `_given_by`, `_channel`, `_withdrawal_channel` (the first three channels), `_one_person`, `_notice_shown` (a notice version unless brought over), `_how` (a method unless brought over), `_recorder` (staff record it, but on the guests' site or brought over), `_withdrawn` (withdrawn ⇔ where it was asked), `_patient` (treatment and reports are a contact's), and `business_consents_check` (the clinic's: withdrawn ⇔ who withdrew it). One active consent per purpose and person: `business_consents_active` (a contact's) and `business_consents_guest_active`; indexes `business_consents_contact`, `business_consents_guest`. Trigger `business_consent_fixed` (function of the same name): never deleted; only the withdrawal is set, once, and a guest's row may move to another guest record (a merge). Trigger `business_consent_rules` (BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE OF `customer_id`): the person is the business's own; not erased; treatment and reports only for a registered patient; never offers for a person marked as a child; a `notice_id` names its own version; without one, only a row brought over (`import`) or a clinic patient's treatment, reports or reminders on the clinic's notice. Trigger `business_consent_guest_copy` (AFTER INSERT OR UPDATE, an offers row of a guest): keeps `customers.marketing_opt_in`, `_at` and `_by` as a copy of the guest's active consent to offers.
Added by: 1791620004000 (as `health_consents`); 1791977000000.

#### `business_children`
Purpose: a person marked as a child, with the parent or guardian whose consent stands for theirs.
Key columns: `contact_id` or `customer_id`, `guardian_name`, `guardian_relation`, `consent_method` (`signed_form`, `verbal`, `digital`), `verified_how` (how the guardian was seen to be the guardian, as told), `marked_by`, `marked_at`, `ended_at`, `ended_by`, `end_reason`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `contact_id`) -> `business_contacts`; `customer_id` -> `customers`; user columns -> `users`.
Rules: CHECKs `business_children_one_person`, `business_children_ended` (ended ⇔ who and why). One mark in force per person: `business_children_contact`, `business_children_guest`; index `business_children_guest_all`. Trigger `business_child_kept`: never deleted; only its end is recorded, once (a guest's may move in a merge). Trigger `business_child_rules` (BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE OF `customer_id`): the person is the business's own, and no consent to offers stands when one is marked.
Added by: 1791977000000.

#### `business_privacy_requests`
Purpose: a request from a person to see, correct or erase their data, or a grievance: when it came, the 90 days to answer it, the answer, and an erasure's record.
Key columns: `request_no` (from 1 per business), `kind` (`access`, `correction`, `erasure`, `grievance`), `contact_id` or `customer_id` (or neither until linked), `person_name`, `reply_to`, `channel` (`counter`, `phone`, `email`, `letter`), `details`, `received_on`, `due_on`, `status` (`open`, `done`, `refused`), `reply`, `closed_at`, `closed_by`, `erased_at`, `erased_by`, `erasure` (JSON: what was erased, what is kept and why, and the erasure's request key), `request_key` + `request_hash`, `created_by`, `created_at`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `contact_id`) -> `business_contacts`; `customer_id` -> `customers`; user columns -> `users`.
Rules: UNIQUE (`business_id`, `id`), (`business_id`, `request_no`), (`business_id`, `request_key`); CHECKs `business_privacy_requests_due` (`due_on = received_on + 90`), `_person` (at most one person), `_closed` (open ⇔ not closed; a closed one has its reply), `_erased` (erased ⇔ who and the record; only an erasure request with its person), `_erasure_done` (an erasure is done only once erased). Partial indexes `business_privacy_requests_open` (business, due date) and `business_privacy_requests_guest`. Trigger `business_privacy_request_rules` (BEFORE INSERT, UPDATE or DELETE): never deleted; a closed request never changes (but a merge's `customer_id`); its kind, number, day received and maker never change; an erasure, once done, stays done; the person is the business's own; never received on a day after `business_privacy_today`.
Added by: 1791977000000.

#### `business_privacy_breaches`
Purpose: the breach register: when a breach was found, what happened, the impact, the steps taken, who is affected, the notice to them, and when the Data Protection Board was told and sent its detailed report (due 72 hours after it was found).
Key columns: `breach_no`, `found_at`, `what_happened`, `impact`, `steps_taken`, `affected` (`all`, `chosen`), `people_notice` (the notice as edited; NULL: filled from the record), `board_informed_at`, `board_report_at`, `board_reference`, `status` (`open`, `closed`), `closed_at`, `closed_by`, `request_key` + `request_hash`, `created_by`, `created_at`, `updated_by`, `updated_at`.
Relations: `business_id` -> `businesses`; user columns -> `users`.
Rules: UNIQUE (`business_id`, `id`), (`business_id`, `breach_no`), (`business_id`, `request_key`); CHECKs `business_privacy_breaches_closed`, `business_privacy_breaches_board` (the Board told and reported to no earlier than found). Trigger `business_privacy_breach_kept`: never deleted; a closed breach never changes; its number and maker never change.
Added by: 1791977000000.

#### `business_privacy_breach_people`
Purpose: the people a breach of chosen people affects.
Key columns: `breach_id`, `contact_id` or `customer_id`, `added_by`, `added_at`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `breach_id`) -> `business_privacy_breaches`; (`business_id`, `contact_id`) -> `business_contacts`; `customer_id` -> `customers`.
Rules: CHECK `business_privacy_breach_people_one_person`; once per breach (`business_privacy_breach_people_contact`, `_guest`); index `business_privacy_breach_people_guest_all`. Trigger `business_privacy_breach_people_rules` (function `business_privacy_breach_person`): the person is the business's own; added or taken off only while the breach is open, and never once a notice went to them; never changed (but a merge's `customer_id`).
Added by: 1791977000000.

#### `business_privacy_breach_notices`
Purpose: each notice sent to a person a breach affects, with its words.
Key columns: `breach_id`, `contact_id` or `customer_id`, `channel` (`whatsapp`, `sms`, `email`, `letter`, `in_person`, `phone`), `notice_text` (50–4000), `sent_by`, `sent_at`, `request_key` + `request_hash`.
Relations: (`business_id`, `breach_id`) -> `business_privacy_breaches`; (`business_id`, `contact_id`) -> `business_contacts`; `customer_id` -> `customers`.
Rules: UNIQUE (`business_id`, `request_key`); CHECK `business_privacy_breach_notices_one_person`; indexes `business_privacy_breach_notices_breach`, `_guest`. Trigger `business_privacy_breach_notices_rules` (function `business_privacy_breach_person`): the person is the business's own; a notice sent never changes or goes (but a merge's `customer_id`).
Added by: 1791977000000.

Also in 1791977000000: `customers_offers_consent_copy` (trigger and function on `customers`, BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE OF the three `marketing_opt_in` columns) refuses any write of the copy but the consent trigger's; the platform module `privacy` and the permission codes `privacy.view`, `privacy.manage`, `privacy.export` in both engines; `business_contacts.erased_at` and `erased_by`; the customer's phone optional on `electronics_repair_jobs` and `electronics_warranties`; `table_requests.privacy_consents` and `privacy_notice_id`. Its Down half gives the clinic `health_consents` back with its patients' rows only (the other consents, and the columns that held them, go), leaves the guest book's copy of the offers consent on `customers` as the consents last had it, and restores the fourteen guards' bodies as they were; it refuses to make the phone required again while a repair job or warranty has none.

## Owner's GST thresholds

Linked sections: [BUSINESS_RULES.md › An owner's GST thresholds](BUSINESS_RULES.md#an-owners-gst-thresholds), [ARCHITECTURE.md §4.7](ARCHITECTURE.md#47-an-owners-gst-thresholds-one-pan-across-both-engines) and [API.md › Owner's GST thresholds](API.md#owners-gst-thresholds). Migration `1791978000000_owner-gst-thresholds.sql`; module code `backend/src/modules/gst-thresholds/`.

**No turnover is stored.** A PAN's turnover is read from each business's own records when asked: a shop's `business_documents` (kind sale, their `subtotal`) less its `business_returns` (their lines' `net`), by `created_at` in `businesses.timezone`; a restaurant's `bills` (not void, their `taxable_amount`) less its `credit_notes` (their `taxable_value`; a refund of kind `credit_note` from before credit notes were written at its bill's share), by `business_days.business_date`, else the date on `restaurant_settings.timezone`. A business's own date is `business_tax_change_today`; its PAN is characters 3 to 12 of its GSTINs (`business_settings.gstin` and its active `business_locations.gstin`; a restaurant's `restaurant_settings.gst_number` when it is a valid GSTIN, and its active `outlets.gst_number`), else `business_gst_pans.pan`. Its owners are its active memberships of the full-access role.

#### `platform_gst_thresholds`
Purpose: platform reference data: each figure that decides what applies to a taxpayer's invoices in a financial year once its aggregate turnover in the year before passed it, with its source. The platform keeps it current by migration, never in the code; no API writes it.
Key columns: `code` (`e_invoicing`, `hsn_digits`, `irp_reporting_days`), `name` and `applies` (in words for people), `turnover` (rupees: 5,00,00,000 or 10,00,00,000), `comparison` (`above`, `at_or_above`), `hsn_digits` (6, for `hsn_digits`), `report_within_days` (30, for `irp_reporting_days`), `applies_from` (the first day it applies; NULL: in force, its first date not recorded, as for `e_invoicing`), `source` (the source and what it says).
Relations: none (no `business_id`: it is the platform's).
Rules: CHECKs on the code's shape, `turnover > 0`, the comparison, `hsn_digits` (4, 6 or 8) and `report_within_days > 0`; UNIQUE NULLS NOT DISTINCT (`code`, `applies_from`) (`platform_gst_thresholds_once`): a later figure for a rule is a row of its own, from its date. Seeded with the three figures and their sources (ClearTax; GSTHero, from 1 April 2021; Taxmann, from 1 April 2025).
Added by: 1791978000000.

#### `business_gst_pans`
Purpose: the PAN an owner records for one of their businesses that has no GSTIN, so its turnover counts with their businesses under that PAN. A business with a GSTIN takes its PAN from it and gets none here. Where the owner is a person, the PAN is personal data: it is shown in full to the business's owners only, and the activity log keeps it masked.
Key columns: `business_id` (the key), `pan` (`^[A-Z]{5}[0-9]{4}[A-Z]$`), `recorded_by`, `recorded_at`.
Relations: `business_id` -> `businesses`; `recorded_by` -> `users`.
Rules: one per business; cleared by deleting the row.
Added by: 1791978000000.

#### `owner_gst_settings`
Purpose: each owner's own warning level: the share of a figure from which their view and the bells of their businesses say the PAN's turnover is nearing it (80% for an owner with no row). Keyed by the owner's login, not by a business: it spans every business they own.
Key columns: `user_id` (the key), `warn_percent` (a whole percent, 50 to 99, default 80), `updated_at`.
Relations: `user_id` -> `users`.
Added by: 1791978000000.

## Business types

The rows of `business_types`: the first twelve created by 1791280000000 multi-business-admin, the other eleven by 1791960000000 more-business-types.

| Code | Name |
|---|---|

## Platform module codes

Core modules are always on and never stored per business; every other module is optional. The restaurant's Features screen lists the restaurant features (see [Restaurant features](#restaurant-features)) and tasks, issues and projects. *Types and default* gives the business types a module applies to (`platform_module_business_types`), which of them start with it switched on, and the types it is recommended for. How to add a module: [ARCHITECTURE.md › Add a module switch](ARCHITECTURE.md#83-add-a-module-switch).

These modules have no permission codes: `restaurant`, `batches`, `serials`, `variants`, `weight` and `books`. Access to them comes from the capability check alone. The restaurant features have none either: the restaurant's own permissions (`roles`, `permissions`) decide who may do what, and the feature decides whether anyone may (see [Restaurant features](#restaurant-features)).

## Business permission codes

Each code is a `<module>.<action>` row of `business_permissions`, listed here by module with the migration that added it. The owner holds every code; the other roles hold what their template grants (`business_role_template_permissions`). How to add one: [ARCHITECTURE.md › Add a permission](ARCHITECTURE.md#84-add-a-permission).

| fuel | `fuel.view` (the board, shifts, tanks and dips, tanker loads, the registers, vehicles and prices; other attendants' shifts and their money with `sales.view`, a load's value with `purchasing.view`, the fleet statements with `sales.view`), `fuel.create` (open and close one's own shift, take the morning dips, record tanker loads; accepting one with `purchasing.create`), `fuel.edit` (open and close any attendant's shift; fleet vehicles and their fill limits), `fuel.approve` (reopen a shift, allow credit over a limit, accept a load outside the density tolerance, correct a dip, record a meter change, cancel a load), `fuel.export`, `fuel.print` | 1791969000000 |
| lodging | `lodging.view` (rooms and their status, the desk, the calendar, reservations, stays and their folios; money with `sales.view`, the occupants' ID details with `lodging.create` or `lodging.print`), `lodging.create` (take and change reservations, check guests in with every occupant and out with the bill, post charges, run the night audit, mark Form-III reports; an advance or a deposit with `sales.edit`), `lodging.edit` (mark rooms clean or dirty, out of order and back), `lodging.approve` (change a guest in the house's rate, void a charge, check a guest out with the bill left to pay), `lodging.cancel` (cancel reservations and mark no-shows, with a charge), `lodging.refund` (hand back advances and deposits held), `lodging.print` (registration cards and the guest register) | 1791970000000 |
| privacy | `privacy.view` (the privacy notice and its versions, the requests and the breach register), `privacy.manage` (publish the notice, log and answer requests, erase a person's data, keep the breach register), `privacy.export` (see or export everything held about one person). Granted to the Manager template and every Manager role | 1791977000000 |
| hardware_tinting | `hardware_tinting.view` (shades and the tinting board), `hardware_tinting.create` (tinted paint on bills and quotations; tinted and handed over on the board; taking a tinted line back, with the PIN of someone with `sales.refund`), `hardware_tinting.edit` (add, import and change shades; the tinting charge). Granted to Manager (all), Cashier and Stock keeper (view, create), Accountant and Viewer (view) | 1791979050000 |

## Restaurant permission codes

The rows of `permissions`, grouped by the `module` column; the migration that added each code is in brackets. How to add one: [ARCHITECTURE.md › Add a permission](ARCHITECTURE.md#84-add-a-permission).

- platform: `platform.restaurants.manage` [1789636497042]
- restaurant: `restaurant.settings.view`, `restaurant.settings.manage`, `outlets.view`, `outlets.manage` [1789636497042]
- users: `users.view`, `users.manage`, `roles.view`, `roles.manage` [1789636497042]; `audit.view` [1789910000000]
- menu: `menu.view`, `menu.manage` [1789636497042]; `menu.availability` [1789910000000]
- tables: `tables.view`, `tables.manage` [1789636497042]

- payments: `payments.manage` [1789636497042]; `advances.refund` [1791340000001]
- tasks: `tasks.view`, `tasks.create`, `tasks.assign`, `tasks.review`, `tasks.manage`, `issues.view`, `issues.create`, `issues.assign`, `issues.review`, `issues.manage`, `projects.view`, `projects.create`, `projects.manage` [1791620000000]. Granted to Super Admin, Restaurant Admin and Manager; `issues.create` to Cashier, Captain, Kitchen and Store keeper; the three `.view` codes to Accountant.
- kitchen: `kot.manage`, `kds.view` [1789636497042]
- reports: `reports.view` [1789636497042]; `sales.view` [1789910000000]

- operations: `business_day.view`, `business_day.manage` [1789731798732]; `business_day.close` [1789910000000]; `cash.movements` [1790810000000]; `logbook.view`, `logbook.write` [1790850000002]
- purchasing: `suppliers.view`, `suppliers.manage`, `purchasing.view`, `purchasing.manage` [1789733008391]; `purchasing.returns` [1789802692848]; `purchasing.receive`, `purchasing.pay` [1789914000000]; `purchasing.approve` [1790880000000]
